#  `` ds_api Â¶

A pythonic interface to the Java DS-API. It provides tools to interact with Pulse in a programmatic way.

##  `` DS_API_CLASSPATH `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DS_API_CLASSPATH: str = 'com.feedzai.commons.dsapi.'
    

##  `` MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH: str = MODEL_CLASSPATH + 'managedlists.'
    

##  `` MODEL_CLASSPATH `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MODEL_CLASSPATH: str = PULSE_API_CLASSPATH + 'apps.model.'
    

##  `` PULSE_API_CLASSPATH `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PULSE_API_CLASSPATH: str = 'com.feedzai.pulse.api.'
    

##  `` PULSE_HOST_ENV `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PULSE_HOST_ENV = 'DS_API_PULSE_HOST'
    

##  `` PULSE_RMI_PORT_ENV `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PULSE_RMI_PORT_ENV = 'DS_API_PULSE_RMI_PORT'
    

##  `` USER_ID_ENV `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    USER_ID_ENV = 'DS_API_USER'
    

##  `` USER_NAME_ENV `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    USER_NAME_ENV = 'DS_API_USER_NAME'

Title: String Indexing - DS API
============================================================

# String IndexingÂ¶

## What is String Indexing?Â¶

String Indexing comes in many forms but in all cases it boils down to pre-processing data to create new Machine Learning model feature(s) from raw string/categorical/text field(s). The generated features are always numerical – either scalars or vectors.

Pulse and the `string_indexing` library provide support for Frequency Indexing. In some circles this is also known as **Count Indexing**.

There are two steps to use (frequency) indexers in Pulse:

  1. **Fit** \- Produce an indexer from train data using the `string_indexing` DS-API library:


    
    
    indexer = string_indexing.fit(train_data, feature_name)
    

2\. **Transform** \- Transform input data using that indexer to add the new feature: Performed inside Pulse plans through the new `FrequencyIndexerUDF`. 

## About Frequency IndexingÂ¶

Frequency Indexing is one of the simplest yet surprisingly effective forms of Indexing.

The pros of Frequency Indexing are its simplicity in terms of tweaking and robustness to over-fit when compared with other forms of indexing. Internal Feedzai benchmarks show recall gains of 8-15pp or 20-40%.

Say you have a string field with the country. Instead of defining the possible values of a categorical field, you can use Frequency Indexing.

The first step is to `fit` a new indexer with a train dataset that includes that field. This will:

  1. Compute the number of occurrences of all the categories in that dataset.
  2. Rank them in descending number of occurrences. The most frequent value will become 0, the second most frequent 1, and so on and so forth.
  3. Store that mapping (string -> rank) and default output value for unknown input values.



What if you have values in the test dataset that you haven't seen before?

That's expected. Let's call those the "other" values. The Frequency Indexer will map those to the integer N, where N is the total number of categories stored in the indexer mapping.

This ensures the output value of the indexer is well defined even for unknown values.

## Indexer clipping and good Data Science modeling practicesÂ¶

Is there a possibility your indexer will eventually receive new or unforeseen values?

If so, you should train your **Machine Learning model** by clipping your indexers first so that the indexed train dataset has "other" values that the indexer will convert to N. These train examples will make the model generalize better for new "other" values observed while predicting.

### How to perform clippingÂ¶

Two forms of clipping are supported:

  * Max categories (**M**): During the indexer fit step, only the **M** most frequent categories from the train data are kept in the indexer.
  * Min samples per category (**S**): Any category/string seen less than **S** times during the indexer fit is dropped from the indexer.



These parameters can be tuned, but sensible values are already used by default, which work well for most scenarios.

Note

Although it can be tweaked, Pulse's `FrequencyIndexerUDF` will by default clip the indexer to **90%** of its input categories. This means that even if you forget to tweak **M** and **S** , the pre-processing pipeline already prevents over-fit and ensures that your Machine Learning model's train and evaluation data will contain "other" values.

## WorkflowÂ¶

The whole user journey when relying on indexing is:

  1. Create model with Indexed features in the Data Science environment:
  2. Create your Pulse indexer(s) using the `string_indexing` DS-API module.
  3. Go to your Pulse Plan and using that Pulse Indexer and the new Pulse `FrequencyIndexerUDF` convert a raw field to an indexed feature.
  4. Train a Machine Learning model with those features.
  5. Put the model (and indexers) in production 

To put the model in another environment, carry the model, features plan and the lists associated to the indexers. Indexers are currently stored as Pulse Lists whose name starts with prefix `"{StringIndexer}"`. Hence, follow the usual procedure to copy lists across environments.

Title: Quickstart - DS API
============================================================

# Quickstart

The modules provided by the Python DS-API package provide convenient utilities that allow interacting with Pulse and performing common data science tasks. This is a list of the available libraries:

  * [`ds_api`](../../reference/#ds_api "            ds_api"). A pythonic interface for Pulse using [pyjnius](https://github.com/kivy/pyjnius). This library is the entry point to interacting programmatically with Pulse. It provides tools that connect to Pulse and perform common data science operations, such as accessing data sources, running plans, training and evaluating models, etc.

  * [`ds_api.exploration`](../../reference/exploration/#ds_api.exploration "            exploration"). Utilities for efficient exploratory data analysis via PySpark. You can efficiently profile your data, easily generate detailed reports, and quickly access personalized results of the exploration in the form of statistics, validations and visualizations.

  * [`ds_api.sampling`](../../reference/sampling/#ds_api.sampling "            sampling"). Efficient sampling utilities through PySpark. It provides a stratified and a random sampling strategy, but can be easily extended to user defined strategies. It supports smart optimized sampling that preserves the minimal history of entities, so historical profiles can be correctly computed while maximizing the sampling reduction power.




## RecipesÂ¶

The best learning resource to get familiar with the DS-API and its available libraries are the notebooks provided in the JupyterLab environment in Pulse.

### DS-APIÂ¶

The entry point to the DS-API and its functionalities is to connect to the Pulse server and instantiate a [`Feedzai`](../../reference/feedzai/#ds_api.feedzai.Feedzai "            Feedzai") object:
    
    
    from ds_api import Feedzai, Server
    
    PULSE_SERVER_ADDRESS = "localhost"
    PULSE_SERVER_PORT = 1099  # Usual pulse.manager.rmi.localregistryport port. Confirm this in Admin UI, inside Pulse configuration.
    pulse = Server.connect(address=PULSE_SERVER_ADDRESS, port=PULSE_SERVER_PORT)
    feedzai = Feedzai(pulse=pulse)
    

Now you can connect to a Pulse application, and for instance list its available data sources:
    
    
    app = feedzai.app(name="app-name")
    app.pullDataSources()
    # ["data source1", "data source2"]
    

To analyze a datasource:
    
    
    DATASOURCE = "data source1"
    datasource = app.pullDataSourceByDesc(DATASOURCE)
    datasource.analyze()
    

To convert a Pulse datasource to a PySpark DataFrame:
    
    
    spark_session = feedzai.get_spark_session()
    data = feedzai.pulse_to_pyspark(spark_session, app, DATASOURCE)
    

More detailed demonstrations on how to use the DS-API can be found in the following notebooks that are available in the JupyterLab integration with Pulse:

  * **01_getting_started.ipynb** \- shows the first steps of using the DS-API: loading data from/to Pulse and converting data to a format that you can use in JupyterLab.
  * Demonstration of how to interact with Pulse's data sources, plans and models using the DS-API:

    * **01_datasources.ipynb**
    * **02_plans.ipynb**
    * **03_models.ipynb**
  * **04_data_loading.ipynb** \- shows how to use the DS-API to programmatically load data into Pulse.




* * *

### ExplorationÂ¶

Continuing from the example in the previous section. To profile a PySpark DataFrame for exploration:
    
    
    from ds_api.exploration import compute_stats
    
    TIMESTAMP_FIELD = "timestamp"
    COST_FIELD = "amount"
    TARGET_FIELD = "fraud"
    TARGET_VALUE = "true"
    TARGET = (TARGET_FIELD, TARGET_VALUE)
    
    results = compute_stats(
        data=data,
        timestamp_field=TIMESTAMP_FIELD,
        target=TARGET,
        cost_field=COST_FIELD
    )
    

Now you can display the exploration report (in JupyterLab) and save it:
    
    
    from IPython.core.display import HTML
    
    HTML(results.report)
    results.save_report_html("report.html")
    

You can also quickly access statistics and visualizations using a convenient API. Some examples:
    
    
    results.time_dependencies
    results[COST_FIELD].quantile()
    results[TARGET_FIELD].frequencies()
    results[TIMESTAMP_FIELD].plot_with_fraud_rate()
    

The notebook **05_data_exploration.ipynb** , available in Pulse's JupyterLab environment, provides a more exhaustive guide to the exploration library.

* * *

### SamplingÂ¶

Continuing from the examples in the previous sections. First initialize the desired sampler:
    
    
    sampler = StratifiedRandomUndersampler(
        target_field=TARGET_FIELD,
        target_value=TARGET_VALUE,
        balance=0.9,
    )
    

Create a filter if you want filter the events that will be marked by the sampling strategy:
    
    
    from pyspark.sql import functions as F
    
    EVENT_TYPE_FIELD = "event_type_field"
    SCORABLE_EVENTS = ["scorable_event_type1", "scorable_event_type2"]
    scorable_events_filter = F.col(EVENT_TYPE_FIELD).isin(SCORABLE_EVENTS)
    

Now you can sample the PySpark DataFrame. In this example the minimal history of the given entity field is preserved in the output dataset, so historical profiles can correctly be computed:
    
    
    ENTITY_FIELD = "card_id"
    MAX_WINDOW_SIZE = 30  # days
    
    sampled_data = sampler.sample(
        train_data,
        filter_expr=scorable_events_filter,
        entity_field=ENTITY_FIELD,
        timestamp_field=TIMESTAMP_FIELD,
        recovery_days=MAX_WINDOW_SIZE
    )
    

A more detailed illustration on how to use the sampling tools for model training and testing purposes is available in Pulse's JupyterLab environment via the notebook **06_data_sampling.ipynb**.

Title: action_param - DS API

#  `` action_param Â¶

##  `` ActionParamTypeEnum Â¶

Bases: `[JavaEnumWrapper](../java_enum_wrapper/#ds_api.java_enum_wrapper.JavaEnumWrapper "            JavaEnumWrapper \(ds_api.java_enum_wrapper.JavaEnumWrapper\)")`

The type of parameters of a rules project action parameter.

This allows the user that defines an action to characterize the parameters with a type. With this type, the screens that allow users to trigger an action will also be able to have an optimized UI to provide a better UX.

###  `` EXPRESSION `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    EXPRESSION = auto()
    

###  `` MANAGED_LIST `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MANAGED_LIST = auto()

Title: datasource - DS API

#  `` datasource Â¶

This module contains the API to interact with Pulse data sources.

Start by fetching a data source by running
    
    
    ds = app.pullDataSourceByDesc("<name as it appears in the UI>")
    

Then you can launch analyze jobs, run feature selection, call AutoML to generate features and more.

##  `` FieldType `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    FieldType = autoclass('com.feedzai.pulse.esp.FieldType')
    

##  `` PARAMS_CSV_ALLOWED `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PARAMS_CSV_ALLOWED = {'has_header': bool, 'sep': str, 'encoding': str, 'missing_values': Set, 'quote': str, 'line_separator': str}
    

##  `` PARAMS_INFERENCE_ALLOWED `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PARAMS_INFERENCE_ALLOWED = {'cat_threshold': int, 'job_properties': Dict, 'n_records': int, 'dtypes': Dict}
    

##  `` PARAMS_PARQUET_ALLOWED `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PARAMS_PARQUET_ALLOWED = {'merge_schemas': bool}
    

##  `` PARQUET_ALLOWED_OVERWRITING_MAP `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    PARQUET_ALLOWED_OVERWRITING_MAP: dict[str, dict] = {value: {TYPE: ([CategoricalField](../types/#ds_api.types.CategoricalField "            CategoricalField \(ds_api.types.CategoricalField\)"), [DateTimeField](../types/#ds_api.types.DateTimeField "            DateTimeField \(ds_api.types.DateTimeField\)")), TYPE: ([StringField](../types/#ds_api.types.StringField "            StringField \(ds_api.types.StringField\)"),)}, value: {TYPE: ([CategoricalField](../types/#ds_api.types.CategoricalField "            CategoricalField \(ds_api.types.CategoricalField\)"), [DateTimeField](../types/#ds_api.types.DateTimeField "            DateTimeField \(ds_api.types.DateTimeField\)")), TYPE: ([NumericalField](../types/#ds_api.types.NumericalField "            NumericalField \(ds_api.types.NumericalField\)"),), TYPE: ([NumericalField](../types/#ds_api.types.NumericalField "            NumericalField \(ds_api.types.NumericalField\)"),)}, value: {TYPE: ([CategoricalField](../types/#ds_api.types.CategoricalField "            CategoricalField \(ds_api.types.CategoricalField\)"), [DateTimeField](../types/#ds_api.types.DateTimeField "            DateTimeField \(ds_api.types.DateTimeField\)")), TYPE: ([NumericalField](../types/#ds_api.types.NumericalField "            NumericalField \(ds_api.types.NumericalField\)"),)}}
    

##  `` logger `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    logger = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` DataSource Â¶
    
    
    DataSource(pyjnius_java)
    

Bases: `[JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")`

Represents a Data Source in Pulse.

###  `` schema `property` Â¶
    
    
    schema: [Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")
    

The schema from the data source.

###  `` slug `property` Â¶
    
    
    slug: str
    

Sanitized name. Equivalent to Pulse 'internal name'.

###  `` subclasses `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    subclasses: List[[JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")] = []
    

###  `` analyze Â¶
    
    
    analyze(default_breakdowns: bool = True, wait: bool = False, job_profile: Optional[[JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)")] = None, job_properties: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None) -> bool
    

Analyze this datasource with Pulse.

By analyzing a data source, Pulse will launch a job to compute approximate histograms for each numerical feature.

The default analysis configuration will also include histogram breakdowns with **all categorical variables**. For each categorical variable in the data source, there will be a new histogram in each numerical feature.

data sources with many categorical fields

It is recommended to set `default_breakdowns=False` when there are many categorical fields. Otherwise, the amount of metadata that is generated might compromise the performance of Pulse.

The datasource analysis will be run with Pulse's default config for datasource analysis.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `default_breakdowns` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Whether the analysis should include breakdowns for all categorical fields or not. Default is `True`. |  `True`  
  
####  `wait` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Whether to wait for the datasource analysis to finish. The data analysis outcome will be logged. Default is `False`. |  `False`  
  
####  `job_profile` Â¶

|  `[JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)")` |  Pulse UI custom job profile. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_properties` Â¶

|  `dict` |  Additional spark properties, e.g. `{"spark.executor.memory": "1g"}`. By default, no additional spark properties are added. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`bool` |  Whether the analysis started or not.  
  
###  `` create `classmethod` Â¶
    
    
    create(app: [App](../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), name: str, path: str, file_format: str = 'csv', schema: [Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None, **kwargs)
    

Creates a new data source in Pulse.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)")` |  The App where the datasource will be created. |  _required_  
  
####  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the data source as it appears in the UI. |  _required_  
  
####  `path` Â¶

|  `str` |  The path of the file containing the dataset. |  _required_  
  
####  `file_format` Â¶

|  `str` |  The input file format. Options are `csv` and `parquet`. Defaults to `csv`. |  `'csv'`  
  
####  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  The Pulse Schema to use. Optional. If not specified, the Schema will be inferred by Pulse. Only used for csv files. |  `None`  
  
####  `kwargs` Â¶

|  `Dict` |  Keyword arguments used for the configuration of the DataSource and for the SchemaInference job. |  `{}`  
  
Other Parameters:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`has_header` |  `bool` |  Flags whether the dataset is to be read with header. Only used for csv files. Defaults to True.  
`sep` |  `str` |  Delimiter to use. Should contain exactly one character. Only used for csv files. Defaults to `','`.  
`missing_values` |  `set of str` |  Set of strings with values for empty value. Only used for csv files. Defaults to `{'', 'NA', 'NULL'}`.  
`quote` |  `str` |  The character used to denote the start and end of a quoted item. Only used for csv files. Defaults to `"'"`.  
`line_separator` |  `str` |  The string used mark the end of line. Only used for csv files. Defaults to `'\n'`.  
`encoding` |  `str` |  Encoding to use for UTF when reading/writing. Only used for csv files. Defaults to `'UTF-8'`.  
`cat_threshold` |  `int` |  A value that represents the maximum number of distinct values on a given field for it to be inferred as categorical. Defaults to 15.  
`job_properties` |  `Dict` |  The dictionary with the specific properties for the schema inference job. Will only be used if no Schema is specified. Defaults to Pulse PropertiesProfile.  
`n_records` |  `int` |  The number of records that will be read per partition when performing datasource schema inference. Will only be used if no Schema is specified. Defaults to 15000.  
`dtypes` |  ` Dict` |  The dictionary with the field overriding information when inferring the datasource schema. Will only be used if no Schema is specified. Defaults to None.  
`merge_schemas` |  `bool` |  Specify if schema merging should be allowed. Only used for parquet files. Defaults to False. See: [https://spark.apache.org/docs/latest/sql-data-sources-parquet.html#schema-merging]()  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataSource` |  DataSource object corresponding to the Pulse datasource created.  
  
###  `` create_automl_fe_plan Â¶
    
    
    create_automl_fe_plan(semantics: Sequence[[SemanticField](../datasources/datasource_transformations/#ds_api.datasources.datasource_transformations.SemanticField "            SemanticField
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.datasources.datasource_transformations.SemanticField\)")], timestamp_field: str, realtime_windows: Optional[Sequence[[TimeModel](../types/#ds_api.types.TimeModel "            TimeModel \(ds_api.types.TimeModel\)")]] = None, scheduled_windows: Optional[Sequence[[TimeModel](../types/#ds_api.types.TimeModel "            TimeModel \(ds_api.types.TimeModel\)")]] = None, plan_name: Optional[str] = None, profile_filter: Optional[str] = None, compute_unfiltered_metrics: bool = False) -> [JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")
    

Creates automl's feature engineering plan.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `semantics` Â¶

|  `sequence of SemanticField` |  Sequence of semantic objects to define the transformations. |  _required_  
  
####  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the timestamp field. |  _required_  
  
####  `realtime_windows` Â¶

|  `sequence of TimeModel` |  Realtime windows to use in the metrics. |  `None`  
  
####  `scheduled_windows` Â¶

|  `sequence of TimeModel` |  Scheduled windows to use in the metrics. |  `None`  
  
####  `plan_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the resulting feature engineering plan. In case it is not provided, a name is automatically generated. |  `None`  
  
####  `profile_filter` Â¶

|  `str` |  Filter to be used in metrics. Fields must be enclosed with the pattern ${field}. |  `None`  
  
####  `compute_unfiltered_metrics` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Boolean that controls if unfiltered metrics are also computed, in case a profile filter is defined. Default is False. |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`[JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")` |  The created plan.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> dataset: DataSource = app.pullDataSourceByDesc("data")
    >>> real_time_windows = (TimeModel(1, TimeUnit.DAY),)
    >>> semantics = (
    ...     TaggedField("card", tags=["card", "grouping-entity"]),
    ...     TaggedField("ip", tags=["ip", "entity", "grouping-entity"]),
    ...     TaggedField("amount", tags=["amount"]),
    ...     CompositeField(
    ...         "card, customer", tags=["grouping-entity"], fields=["card", "customer"]
    ...     ),
    ... )
    >>> plan = dataset.create_automl_fe_plan(
    ...     semantics=semantics,
    ...     timestamp_field="timestamp1",
    ...     realtime_windows=real_time_windows,
    ...     plan_name="test fe plan",
    ...     profile_filter="${event_type} == 'TRANSACTION' and ${scorable} == true"
    ... )

###  `` run_automl_feature_engineering Â¶
    
    
    run_automl_feature_engineering(semantics: Sequence, timestamp_field: str = 'event_occurred_at', realtime_windows: Optional[Sequence[[TimeModel](../types/#ds_api.types.TimeModel "            TimeModel \(ds_api.types.TimeModel\)")]] = None, scheduled_windows: Optional[Sequence[[TimeModel](../types/#ds_api.types.TimeModel "            TimeModel \(ds_api.types.TimeModel\)")]] = None, plan_name: Optional[str] = None, output_datasource_name: Optional[str] = None, profile_filter: Optional[str] = None, compute_unfiltered_metrics: bool = False, output_path: Optional[str] = None) -> str
    

Runs automl's feature engineering on this datasource.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `semantics` Â¶

|  `sequence of SemanticField` |  Sequence of semantic objects to define the transformations. |  _required_  
  
####  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the timestamp field. |  `'event_occurred_at'`  
  
####  `realtime_windows` Â¶

|  `sequence of TimeModel` |  Realtime windows to use in the metrics. |  `None`

|  `sequence of TimeModel` |  Scheduled windows to use in the metrics. |  `None`  
  
####  `plan_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the resulting feature engineering plan. In case it is not provided, a name is automatically generated. |  `None`  
  
####  `output_datasource_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the feature engineered datasource. In case it is not provided, the process created a derived name from the input source. |  `None`  
  
####  `profile_filter` Â¶

|  `str` |  Filter to be used in metrics. Fields must be enclosed with the pattern ${field}. |  `None`  
  
####  `compute_unfiltered_metrics` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Boolean that controls if unfiltered metrics are also computed, in case a profile filter is defined. Default is False. |  `False`

Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> dataset: DataSource = app.pullDataSourceByDesc("data")
    >>> real_time_windows = (TimeModel(1, TimeUnit.DAY),)
    >>> semantics = (
    ...     TaggedField("card", tags=["card", "grouping-entity"]),
    ...     TaggedField("ip", tags=["ip", "entity", "grouping-entity"]),
    ...     TaggedField("amount", tags=["amount"]),
    ...     CompositeField(
    ...         "card, customer",
    ...         tags=["grouping-entity"], fields=["card", "customer"]
    ...     ),
    ... )
    >>> plan_execution_id = dataset.run_automl_feature_engineering(
    ...     semantics=semantics,
    ...     timestamp_field="timestamp1",
    ...     realtime_windows=real_time_windows,
    ...     plan_name="test fe plan",
    ...     output_datasource_name="output data",
    ...     profile_filter="${event_type} == 'TRANSACTION' and ${scorable} == true"
    ... )
    

###  `` run_feature_selection Â¶
    
    
    run_feature_selection(target_field: str, threshold: float = 0.9, sample_size: int = 200000, max_features: int | None = None, job_properties: dict[str, str] | None = None, job_profile: [JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)") | None = None)
    

Run feature selection on the Datasource.

Features are selected so that the cumulative sum of the feature importance on the selected features is below the given `threshold`. A maximum of `max_features` will be returned. The feature importance is computed from an H2O GBT model run on a sample of the data.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `target_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  The field to be used as target. |  _required_  
  
####  `threshold` Â¶

|  `float` |  Threshold on the cumulative feature importance, defaults to 0.9. |  `0.9`  
  
####  `sample_size` Â¶

|  `int` |  Sample size, defaults to 200,000. |  `200000`  
  
####  `max_features` Â¶

|  `int` |  Maximum number of features to return, defaults to Inf. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_properties` Â¶

|  `Mapping of str` |  Map of spark configurations for the job. If not provided defaults to the config of the current spark session. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_profile` Â¶

|  `[JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)")` |  Pulse UI custom job profile. |  `None`  
Notes

If both job_properties and job_profile are specified and there is intersection between their properties, the job_properties property will overwrite the job_profile property.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`set of str` |  The selected features.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> selected_features = datasource.run_feature_selection(
    ...     target_field="target"
    ... )
    >>> job_profile = app.pullJobProfileByDesc("job_profile_name")
    >>> selected_features = datasource.run_feature_selection(
    ...     target_field="target",
    ...     threshold=0.7,
    ...     sample_size=1_000_000,
    ...     max_features=20,
    ...     job_properties={
    ...         "spark.master": "yarn",
    ...         "spark.app.name": "Feature Selection",
    ...         "spark.executor.memory": "1g",
    ...         "spark.dynamicAllocation.enabled": "True",
    ...         "spark.dynamicAllocation.maxExecutors": "4",
    ...     }
    ... )
    >>> selected_features = datasource.run_feature_selection(
    ...     target_field="target",
    ...     threshold=0.7,
    ...     sample_size=1_000_000,
    ...     max_features=20,
    ...     job_profile=job_profile
    ... )
    

###  `` split Â¶
    
    
    split(plan_name: str, splits: Set[[Split](../datasources/datasource_transformations/#ds_api.datasources.datasource_transformations.Split "            Split
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.datasources.datasource_transformations.Split\)")], timestamp_field: str = 'event_occurred_at', scorable_filter: Optional[str] = None, wait: bool = False) -> Set[str]
    

Splits the datasource according to the splits passed.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `plan_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name that the resulting datasource split plan will have. |  _required_  
  
####  `splits` Â¶

|  `set of Split` |  A set of `Split` that will determine how the data source will be split. Must have at least one element. |  _required_

####  `scorable_filter` Â¶

|  `str` |  Optional string to filter scorable events. Fields must be enclosed with the pattern ${field}. |  `None`  
  
####  `wait` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Optional flag to indicate whether the method should wait for the datasource split to end before returning the designated method output. |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`set of str` |  The names of the new datasources that the splitting process will create.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> scorable = "(${amount} > 0)"
    >>> splits = {
    ...     Split("train", 1350776834512, 1351173363000),
    ...     Split("test", 1351173363000, 1351473463000)
    ... }
    >>> datasources = datasource.split(
    ...     plan_name="plan",
    ...     splits=splits,
    ...     timestamp_field="timestamp",
    ...     scorable_filter=scorable
    ... )
    

###  `` to_pyspark Â¶

#  `` deprecate Â¶

##  `` deprecated_argument Â¶
    
    
    deprecated_argument(removal_version: str, kw_name: str, replacement: Optional[str] = None) -> Callable[[_TFunc], _TFunc]
    

Decorator factory that marks an argument of a function as deprecated.

Returns a decorator that, when applied to a function, checks at call time whether the deprecated keyword argument (`kw_name`) was supplied. If so, a `DeprecationWarning` is issued indicating the argument's pending removal and, optionally, pointing to a replacement.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `removal_version` Â¶

|  `str` |  The version of the library in which the argument is marked as deprecated (e.g., "2.0"). |  _required_  
  
###  `kw_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the argument to be deprecated. |  _required_  
  
###  `replacement` Â¶

|  `str or None` |  Optional name of the replacement argument to suggest to the user. If None, it means the argument is removed entirely. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Callable` |  A decorator that wraps a function and warns when the deprecated keyword argument is used.

###  `` get_feature_importance_results Â¶
    
    
    get_feature_importance_results(target_value: str | bool | int) -> DataFrame
    

Extract the results of the absolute and relative feature importance evaluated in terms of #Recall and $Recall. Feature importance based on $Recall is only available if a cost field has been defined in the model evaluation config.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---

Type | Description

`DataFrame` |  Pandas df with feature importance results, ordered by descending values of the #Recall-based feature importance.  
  
Examples:

###  `` run_feature_importance Â¶
    
    
    run_feature_importance(desired_fpr: float = 0.01, sample_rate: float = 0.3, random_seed: int | None = None, job_profile: [JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)") | None = None, job_properties: dict[str, str] | None = None, wait: bool = False) -> None
    

Run feature importance on the model evaluation object.

The values of the features are shuffled, and the loss in (transaction or cost) Recall with respect to the true Recall is computed, for each feature individually.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `desired_fpr` Â¶

|  `float` |  The desired FPR for feature importance. Defaults to 0.01. |  `0.01`  
  
####  `sample_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  Sampling rate to be applied prior to feature importance. Defaults to 0.3. |  `0.3`  
  
####  `random_seed` Â¶

|  `int` |  Random seed, for reproducibility purposes. Defaults to a random integer between 1 and 1000000. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_profile` Â¶

|  `[JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)")` |  Pulse UI custom job profile. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_properties` Â¶

|  `Mapping of str` |  Map of spark configurations for the job. Defaults to Pulse's Properties Profile. |  `None`  
  
####  `wait` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Waits or not for feature importance to end. Defaults to False. |  `False`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_eval.run_feature_importance()
    >>> # or
    >>> model_eval.run_feature_importance(
    ...     desired_fpr=0.01,
    ...     sample_rate=0.3,
    ...     random_seed=123456
    ... )
    >>> # or
    >>> model_eval.run_feature_importance(
    ...     desired_fpr=0.01,
    ...     sample_rate=0.3,
    ...     random_seed=123456,
    ...     job_properties={"spark.executor.memory": "5g"},
    ... )

###  `` get_spark_local_session `staticmethod` Â¶
    
    
    get_spark_local_session(spark_params: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None, spark_app_name: Optional[str] = None, log_level: SparkLogLevel = ERROR)
    

Get SparkSession configured in local mode. It will use all available CPUs and each executor has 1G RAM and 1 vcore.

Note that Spark maintains a single activate session, thus unless it was crashed, you would always get the same one, but configured in local mode. Even if you stop SparkSession or SparkContext, in pySpark, it will remember previous configurations.

This means the following:

  1. Previously spark had properties A, B, and C defined
  2. You call this function and you will get a SparkSession with A, B, and C defined as well as the local mode



Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `spark_params` Â¶

|  `dict` |  Dictionary with Spark parameters to be overridden or added. Full list of parameters can be found at [https://spark.apache.org/docs/2.3.0/configuration.html](). |  `None`  
  
####  `spark_app_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name for the spark application. If 'None' then one is automatically assigned. |  `None`  
  
####  `log_level` Â¶

|  `SparkLogLevel` |  Log Level of the spark session, defaults to `SparkLogLevel.ERROR`. |  `ERROR`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`SparkSession` |  A Spark Session in local mode, None if spark_params is not a dictionary.  
  
###  `` get_spark_session Â¶
    
    
    get_spark_session(spark_params: Dict[str, str] | None = None, exclude_spark_params: List[str] | None = None, app_name: str | None = None, spark_app_name: str | None = None, job_profile_name: str | None = None, group_name: str | None = None, clean_spark_properties: bool = True, log_level: SparkLogLevel = ERROR, session_type: SparkSessionType = CLASSIC, spark_remote: str | None = None)
    

Get SparkSession with default Pulse's configuration.

Note that Spark maintains a single activate session, thus unless it was crashed, you would always get the same one, but with last Pulse's configuration (and possible old configurations). Even if you stop SparkSession or SparkContext, in pySpark, it will remember previous configurations.

This means the following:

  1. Pulse has properties A, B, and C defined
  2. You call this function and you will get a SparkSession with A, B, and C properties
  3. You remove property A and add property D
  4. You call this function and you will get a SparkSession with **A** , B, C, and D



Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `spark_params` Â¶

|  `dict` |  Dictionary with Spark parameters to be overridden or added. Full list of parameters can be find in https://spark.apache.org/docs/latest/configuration.html |  `None`  
  
####  `exclude_spark_params` Â¶

|  `list` |  List with Spark properties to be removed. Full list of Spark properties can be find in https://spark.apache.org/docs/latest/configuration.html |  `None`  
  
####  `app_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  An optional name of a pulse application. This parameter should be used when we want to have access to application specific properties. This allows to use the same properties being usd by Spark jobs executed in Pulse. |  `None`  
  
####  `spark_app_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name for the spark application. If 'None' then one is automatically assigned. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_profile_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the job profile to reuse the Spark properties defined there. If 'None' then it will retrieve the properties associated to default job profile for jobs of type 'EXTERNAL_DS_JOB'. |  `None`  
  
####  `group_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the group model used to retrieve the configurations defined in its properties. |  `None`  
  
####  `clean_spark_properties` Â¶

|  `bool` |  True if it should clean the Spark properties specific to Pulse use case, false otherwise. |  `True`  
  
####  `log_level` Â¶

|  `SparkLogLevel` |  Log Level of the spark session, defaults to SparkLogLevel.ERROR. |  `ERROR`  
  
####  `session_type` Â¶

|  `SparkSessionType` |  The type of Spark Session to be created, defaults to `SparkSessionType.CLASSIC` |  `CLASSIC`  
  
####  `spark_remote` Â¶

|  `str` |  The URL of the remote Spark Connect server |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`_TSparkSession` |  A SparkSession with Pulse's configuration, None if spark_params is not a dictionary  
  
###  `` pandas_to_pulse `staticmethod` Â¶

The dataframe will be written in the path specified before being added to Pulse. DateType and TimestampType fields are written as unix time (in seconds). The schema will be automatically inferred using AbstractDataSource#inferSchema approach if the Schema is not specified.

All pySpark types but DecimalType and MLlib types are supported.

###  `` run Â¶
    
    
    run(config: Path, ignored_fields: set | None = None, n_trials: int = 100, timeout: float | None = None, n_jobs: int = 1, catch: tuple[Type[Exception], ...] = (), show_progress_bar: bool = False, keep_trained_models: bool = False) -> None

####  `timeout` Â¶

####  `show_progress_bar` Â¶

####  `keep_trained_models` Â¶

`model_type: RandomForestConfig` |

````` |

    >>> study.run(
    ...     config=Path("random_forest_config.yml"),
    ...     ignored_fields={"card", "mcc"},
    ...     n_trials=100,
    ...     n_jobs=4,
    ... )

Title: java_enum_wrapper - DS API
============================================================

#  `` java_enum_wrapper Â¶

##  `` JavaEnumWrapper Â¶

Bases: `str`, `Enum`

Class to represent Java Enums as native Python Enums.

This enum is a proxy for Java Enums. It's preferred to using the pyjnius wrapped object so that we keep the advantages of having the Enum, such as identifying invalid types at compile time, thereby catching bugs early on. Additionally, this decouples the user's code from our Java or Pulse's implementation.

When using this enum, the use of the `auto` function from the enum package is recommended, which will set the value of an attribute be the same as the name. The values of attributes are given to the `JavaEnumWrapper.__new__` method upon attribute creation, which override the attribute's value with Java Enum attribute corresponding to the given value. Therefore, the use of `auto` is recommended, which will assign the value to be the same as the name of the python enum attribute. However, if you want to give any of the attributes a name different from the java counterpart, you can go without using `auto` and assigning the value from the java counterpart to the new name in python.



================================================================================
  PAGE 24 of 97

================================================================================

Source: https://cam.feedzai.com/ds-studio-ds-api/25.0/reference/java_wrapper/
Title: java_wrapper - DS API
============================================================

#  `` java_wrapper Â¶

##  `` logger `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    logger = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` JavaWrapper Â¶
    
    
    JavaWrapper(pyjnius_java)
    

A wrapper for jnius generated objects.

The jnius framework already does a substantial work when converting java objects into python object. Unfortunately, this work is more focused on java native types. This class complements this functionally by providing additional transformation functions that can automatically convert objects from both the Java native library as well as some types from the Feedzai common library. As such, this class should always be used to wrap pyjnius generated objects.

An example of the usage for this class would be: Given a java class Car, which contains a Car#getWheels which returns a java.util.List. A JavaWrapper for an instance of this class would return a python array of Wheel objects (the conversion from java.util.List to a python array would occur automatically). Note that pyjnius only converts java.util.List objects into a pyjnius generated class for java.util.List, thus making the code that interacts with it more verbose.

Note also that the conversions are transitive. A java method that returns a List> is automatically converted into a python array of arrays of strings.

Another useful feature of this class is that it is able to log details for the JVM exceptions thrown from the pyjnius JVM. The details include the java stacktrace, which should be useful to debug java code that pyjnius interacts with.

Class variables "java_classref" and "java_class" are to be defined by every subclass, which refer to the java class that the python class can represent. Imagining for a DataSource, it basically says, if you give me a "com.feedzai.commons.dsapi.DataSource" I can convert it to python. They will be automatically added to a map available to every class, so the return types coming from java can accurately/automatically be converted to their Python friends. In this case, if no good match is found, it defaults to this JavaWrapper class.

Finally, note that if you intend to change the init's signature of your new subclass, be a pal and define a static method named "auto_cast" that must have one parameter: the java object that represents our python counterpart. That way, our auto-cast system will cleverly pick up that method and work smoothly. This avoids extra ".unwrap()" and ".get()" that make the use of the api by the end-user less intuitive.

There are examples of this auto-cast method in the models package and types module.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `pyjnius_java` Â¶

|  |  The original pyjnius object. |  _required_  
  
###  `` subclasses `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    subclasses: List[JavaWrapper] = []

Title: lists - DS API
============================================================

#  `` lists Â¶  
  
DS-API module to manage lists and list items in Pulse.

A List in Pulse is either Simple (`SimpleManagedListModel`) or Complex (`ComplexManagedListModel`). A Pulse List can contain items of the respective type: `SimpleManagedListItemModel` or `ComplexManagedListItemModel`.

Additionally, lists can have tags (`ManagedListTagModel`).

This module also provides a wrapper for `ManagedList`, to simplify list operations like `delete()`, and to provide an API that is more consistent with the rest of the DS-API.

##  `` ComplexManagedListItemModel `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    ComplexManagedListItemModel = autoclass([MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH](../#ds_api.MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH "            MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH

       \(ds_api.MANAGED_LISTS_CLASSPATH\)") + 'ComplexManagedListItemModel')

       \(ds_api.MODEL_CLASSPATH\)") + 'ComplexManagedListModel')

##  `` ManagedList Â¶
    
    
    ManagedList(pyjnius_java)
    

Bases: `[JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")`

Wrapper for Pulse's Lists. Can handle all list types.

Although one can use the Pulse API directly by accessing the methods of the managed list wrapped by this class with `getManagedList()`, this Python wrapper pulls all those methods to the main class.

Any method implemented by `ManagedList` takes precedence over exposed `self.getManagedList()._java_attributes()` members, to allow ManagedList to implement custom behaviour that overrides the wrapped class's members.

###  `` evaluate Â¶
    
    
    evaluate(name: str, input_datasource: [DataSource](../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)"), model_eval_config: [ModelEvalConfig](../model_eval_config/#ds_api.model_eval_config.ModelEvalConfig "            ModelEvalConfig \(ds_api.model_eval_config.ModelEvalConfig\)"), wait: bool = False, job_profile: [JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)") | None = None, job_properties: dict[str, str] | None = None, file_format: str | None = None) -> 'Evaluation'
    

Evaluate the model against input_datasource using model_eval_config, with the option of using the default job profiles, a specific job profile, or custom spark_job_properties.

Note

if both job_properties and job_profile are specified and there is intersection between their properties, the job_properties property will prevail over the job_profile property.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Evaluation name. |  _required_  
  
####  `input_datasource` Â¶

|  `[DataSource](../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)")` |  DataSource to be evaluated. |  _required_  
  
####  `model_eval_config` Â¶

|  `[ModelEvalConfig](../model_eval_config/#ds_api.model_eval_config.ModelEvalConfig "            ModelEvalConfig \(ds_api.model_eval_config.ModelEvalConfig\)")` |  ModelEvalConfig configuration for the model evaluation. |  _required_  
  
####  `wait` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Whether to wait for the model evaluation to finish. The evaluation outcome will be logged. By default, False. |  `False`  
  
####  `job_profile` Â¶

|  `[JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)")` |  Pulse UI custom job profile. |  `None`  
  
####  `job_properties` Â¶

|  `Mapping` |  Map containing spark job configurations. It will apply default configurations (Pulse Properties Profile) and job_properties. |  `None`  
  
####  `file_format` Â¶

|  `str` |  Output file format. When not defined, it will reuse the format of the input data source. Possible options are: "parquet" (preferred) or "csv". |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`[Evaluation](../evaluation/#ds_api.evaluation.Evaluation "            Evaluation \(ds_api.evaluation.Evaluation\)")` |  The model evaluation object.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app : App
    >>> data = app.pullDataSourceByDesc("data")
    >>> model_project = app.pullModelProjectByDesc("model_project")
    >>> model = model_project.pullModelByDesc("model")
    >>> job_profile = app.pullJobProfileByDesc("job_profile_name")
    >>> eval_config = ModelEvalConfig()
    ...
    >>> evaluation = model.evaluate(
    ...     name=str(uuid.uuid4()),
    ...     input_datasource=data,
    ...     model_eval_config=eval_config,
    ... )
    >>> # or
    >>> evaluation = model.evaluate(
    ...     name=str(uuid.uuid4()),
    ...     input_datasource=data,
    ...     model_eval_config=eval_config,
    ...     job_profile=job_profile,
    ... )
    >>> # or
    >>> evaluation = model.evaluate(
    ...     name=str(uuid.uuid4()),
    ...     input_datasource=data,
    ...     model_eval_config=eval_config,
    ...     job_properties={"spark.executor.memory": "5g"},
    ... )
    >>> # or
    >>> evaluation = model.evaluate(
    ...     name=str(uuid.uuid4()),
    ...     input_datasource=data,
    ...     model_eval_config=eval_config,
    ...     job_profile=job_profile,
    ...     job_properties={"spark.executor.memory": "5g"},
    ... )
    

###  `` export_binary Â¶
    
    
    export_binary() -> bytes | None
    

Export the model binary bundle in a ZIP.

The result of this export is a directory with the model ID and its contents inside it. E.g. for a LightGBM model, you should expect to have something akin to:
    
    
    <model ID>/
        LightGBM_model.txt
    

To read its contents you can unzip it by doing
    
    
    import io
    from zipfile import ZipFile
    
    with ZipFile(io.BytesIO(model.export_binary())) as f:
        txt = f.read(f"{model.id}/LightGBM_model.txt").decode("ascii")
    

For arbitrary models, you can search for the files in the bundle by using `ZipFile.namelist`:
    
    
    with ZipFile(io.BytesIO(binary)) as f:
        print(f.namelist())
    

Note

In case the binary fails to export, this method returns None.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`bytes` |  The model binary bundle.

#  `` model_eval_config Â¶

##  `` ModelEvalConfig Â¶
    
    
    ModelEvalConfig(ignored_fields: set[str] | None = None, cost_field: str | None = None, scored_instances_filepath: str | None = None, sink_desc: str | None = None, breakdowns: list[dict[str, set[str]]] | None = None, target_variable: str | None = None, schema: [Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None)
    

Bases: `[JavaWrapper](../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")`

A configuration used for Model evaluation.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `ignored_fields` Â¶

|  `set of str` |  Set of names corresponding to the fields to be ignored when evaluating a model. |  `None`  
  
###  `cost_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Additional cost field used as weight for evaluation metrics. |  `None`  
  
###  `scored_instances_filepath` Â¶

|  `str` |  Path to file where scored instances should be saved. |  `None`  
  
###  `sink_desc` Â¶

|  `str` |  The sink name for the model evaluation datasource. If not provided the new datasource will take the evaluation's name. |  `None`  
  
###  `breakdowns` Â¶

|  `list of mappings of (str-> {str...})` |  Each element of the list represents a breakdown analysis. Each element in the contained mapping represents a breakdown field, with  -> . |  `None`  
  
###  `target_variable` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the target variable to be used in the model evaluation. This is required for unsupervised models and optional for supervised models. When provided for supervised models, it overrides the model's target variable. The target variable must be a binary categorical field (exactly 2 values) from the evaluation datasource and should be included in ignored_fields. |  `None`  
  
###  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> import ds_api
    >>> model: ds_api.Model    # Train model with a ds_api.ModelProject
    >>> # Define ModelEvalConfig w/ breakdown per value of `caucasian`:
    >>> test_datasource: ds_api.DataSource
    >>> eval_config = ds_api.ModelEvalConfig(
    ...     breakdowns=[
    ...         {"Caucasian": {"true", "false"}},
    ...         # {"Age Group": {"-25", "26-46", "46-65", "66-"}},
    ...         # ...
    ...     ],
    ...     schema=test_datasource.getSchema(),
    ... )
    >>> evaluation = model.evaluate("test_eval", test_datasource, eval_config)
    >>> evaluation.waitUntilEvaluationFinished()
    >>> (
    ...     (_, global_results),
    ...     ((non_caucasian_col_val,), non_caucasian_results),
    ...     ((caucasian_col_val,), caucasian_results),
    ... ) = evaluation.getResultForBreakdowns("true")
    >>> assert non_caucasian_col_val == "false" and caucasian_col_val == "true"

    breakdowns

The target variable must be a categorical field present in the evaluation datasource and should be included in ignored_fields. |  `None`  
  
####  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`ModelEvalConfig` |  A model evaluation configuration.

###  `` train Â¶
    
    
    train(name: str, input_datasource: [DataSource](../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)"), target: str, model_config: [TrainingConfig](../models/#ds_api.models.TrainingConfig "            TrainingConfig \(ds_api.models.TrainingConfig\)"), job_profile: [JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)") | None = None, job_properties: dict[str, str] | None = None, wait: bool = False) -> [Model](../model/#ds_api.model.Model "            Model \(ds_api.model.Model\)")
    

Creates and trains a given model with TrainingConfig using DataSource with target label field, with the option of using the default job profiles, a specific job profile, or custom spark_job_properties.

Note: if both job_properties and job_profile are specified and there is intersection between their properties, the job_properties property will prevail over the job_profile property.

####  `job_properties` Â¶

|  `Mapping` |  Map containing spark job configurations. It will apply default configurations (Pulse Properties Profile) and job_properties. |  `None`  
  
####  `wait` Â¶

###  `` auto_rename_metrics Â¶
    
    
    auto_rename_metrics()
    

Automatically renames all the metrics based on their configuration.

Default naming: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_{aggregation}_ {aggregation fields}_{window size}_{is_real_time})` eg. accountid_avg_amount_1h_rt. Advanced aggregations will be named: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_agg_ {window size}_{is_real_time})`. If a metric name is duplicated anywhere in the plan it appends "_n". Updates the references to the old names across the plan.

###  `` diff Â¶
    
    
    diff(other: Plan, show: bool = True, only_diff: bool = True, save: bool = False, save_as: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[Plan]
    

Compare 2 plans. Differences can be displayed graphically when running inside a Jupyter Notebook.

Note

When creating a new plan from the diff, the schema and timestamp fields must be the in both plans. Also note that a renamed operator acts like a new operator for the diff, which means an operator that is not present in the 'other' plan will be discarded.

Danger

List and Sample operators are not supported.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `other` Â¶

|  `Plan` |  Plan to compare against. The 'new' plan. When pushing the diff to Pulse, this is the same as doing `other - self`. |  _required_  
  
####  `show` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Whether to display the differences as an HTML output. Only possible within a Jupyter Notebook. Defaults to True. |  `True`  
  
####  `only_diff` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If True only shows features with differences. |  `True`  
  
####  `save` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If True it outputs a new valid plan with the differences. This will override 'show'. Defaults to False. |  `False`  
  
####  `save_as` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the diff plan, if None it will be `"{plan_a}_diff_{plan_b}"`. |  `None`  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`NotImplementedError` |  When trying to show a diff outside a Jupyter Notebook.  
`ValueError` |  When the plans do not have the same schema or a different timestamp field. This is only raised when 'return_diff_plan' is set to true.

###  `` from_csv `classmethod` Â¶
    
    
    from_csv(app: [App](../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), schema: [Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None, timestamp_field_desc: str | None = None, name: str | None = None, path: Path | str | None = None, *, main_channel: [Channel](../plans/omnichannel/#ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel "            Channel
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel\)") | None = None, secondary_channels: list[[Channel](../plans/omnichannel/#ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel "            Channel
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel\)")] | None = None) -> Plan
    

Returns a new Deployable Plan with the configuration from the csv file.

The new Plan configuration will be updated in Pulse.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)")` |  Application ds_api object. |  _required_  
  
####  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pulse schema |  `None`  
  
####  `timestamp_field_desc` Â¶

|  `str` |  Desc name of the schema timestamp field. |  `None`  
  
####  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the new plan |  `None`  
  
####  `path` Â¶

|  `str or Path` |  Path to where the csv will be read from. |  `None`  
  
####  `main_channel` Â¶

|  `[Channel](../plans/omnichannel/#ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel "            Channel


  
      dataclass
   \(ds_api.plans.omnichannel.Channel\)")` |  If a main_channel is given, the schema, and timestamp_field_desc parameters are ignored. |  `None`  
  
####  `secondary_channels` Â¶

|  `list of Channel` |  Optionally pass the omnichannel configuration for the plan. Note that it is assumed the "schema" and "timestamp_field_desc" are properties of the main channel. |  `None`  
Notes

This method is intended to edit an existing Plan configuration previously exported.

General guidelines to edit the csv:

  * The row order of the 'operator_name' will determine their position in the Pulse Plan.
  * Pulse fields must be enclosed within the pattern ${field}.
  * Boolean values: {True, "TRUE", "True", "true", "T", "t", "1", 1} are accepted.
  * Any feature to be removed should be on a single row and the field 'map_remove_fields' should be True.
  * Feature updates will occur if a feature name is repeated.



Fields input format and considerations:

  * A more extensive documentation on each of the fields can be found on the Feature class docstrings.
  * operator_type: possible values are: map, metric, list, filter.
  * expression: to be used for Filter, Map and List operators.
  * metric_groupby_fields: formatted as a list of strings, eg. `['MerchantName', 'City']`.
  * metric_aggregation: Pulse fields must be enclosed within the pattern `'${field_desc}'`. eg. `'avg(${amount})'`, `'countDistinct(${MerchantCity})'`, `'count()'`.
  * metric_window_size: usual types "second", "minute", "hour", "day", "week". eg. 1 day.
  * metric_is_real_time: boolean.
  * metric_filter: Fields must be enclosed with the pattern `'${field}'` eg. `'${TransactionAmount} > 100 and ${Fraud} != false`'.
  * metric_delay: int.
  * metric_is_tumbling: boolean.
  * metric_filter_incomplete_instances: boolean, must be the same for the whole operator.
  * metric_timestamp_field: must be the same for the whole operator.
  * map_field_type: possible values are: numerical, string, datetime, categorical.
  * map_field_config: contains additional field configuration for datetime fields, possible values: nanoseconds, microseconds, milliseconds, seconds, minutes, hours, days. Or categories for categorical fields, eg. `['true', 'false']`.
  * map_remove_fields: boolean.
  * list_operation: get, or contains.
  * list_field: desc of the list field.



Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Plan` |  New Plan  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Exception` |  If the Plan configuration is invalid.

###  `` get_feature_dependencies Â¶
    
    
    get_feature_dependencies(feature: [BaseFeature](../plans/features/feature/#ds_api.plans.features.feature.BaseFeature "            BaseFeature \(ds_api.plans.BaseFeature\)"), ascending=True) -> set[[FeatureUID](../plans/utils/#ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID "            FeatureUID \(ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID\)")]

Feature dependencies are represented as a unique tuple of feature name and operator name. Considers the filter operator as a feature with dependencies.

###  `` get_features_dependencies_by_type Â¶
    
    
    get_features_dependencies_by_type() -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Set[str]]]
    

Gets a set of feature dependencies calculated recursively.

Feature dependencies are represented as a unique tuple of feature name and operator name.

Considers the filter operator as a feature with dependencies.

If we have the following graph of dependencies for features A, B and C and specific dependency types:

[C]--(map)-->[B]--(aggregation)-->[A]

Feature C is considered an "aggregation" dependency of Feature A (regardless of its dependency type to B) because it is a dependency of the intermediate Feature B that is used in the aggregation expression of Feature A.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Dict[str, Dict[str, Set[str]]]` |  Dictionary of features' dependencies by type. The keys are the names of the features and the values are the dicts of upstream dependencies by type.  
  
###  `` get_features_dependencies_graph Â¶
    
    
    get_features_dependencies_graph() -> DiGraph
    

Gets a directed graph object from NetworkX built with the dependencies between the Plan features.

Nodes are unique tuples of feature and operator names.

Considers the filter operator as a feature with dependencies.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DiGraph` |  DiGraph

    run(input_datasources: list[[DataSource](../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)")], output_names: list[str], output_paths: list[str], *, secondary_datasources: dict[str, [DataSource](../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)")] | None = None, job_profile: [JobProfile](../job_profile/#ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile "            JobProfile \(ds_api.job_profile.JobProfile\)") | None = None, job_properties: dict[str, str] | None = None, wait: bool = False, file_format: str = 'parquet', partitioning_config: [PartitioningConfig](../partitioning_config/#ds_api.partitioning_config.PartitioningConfig "            PartitioningConfig \(ds_api.partitioning_config.PartitioningConfig\)") = [AdaptivePartitioningConfig](../adaptive_partitioning_config/#ds_api.adaptive_partitioning_config.AdaptivePartitioningConfig "            AdaptivePartitioningConfig \(ds_api.adaptive_partitioning_config.AdaptivePartitioningConfig\)")(), **kwargs: bool | str | set[str]) -> str

#  `` plans Â¶  
  
Plans subpackage containing the implementation of features, operators and utilities to enable the programmatic manipulation of Plans.

Omnichannel Plans

At the moment Omnichannel integration is still in beta. Feature dependencies are unstable for Omnichannel plans.

    scores: Optional[List[float]]
    

Get or set an optional List that, if present, contains the scores that this rule should set when triggered.

The order in which the scores are present in the list should correspond to the order in which the possible target values are configured in the {@link AbstractRulesProjectModel#getSchema()}. The scores are expected to all be between Rule.model.MIN_RULE_SCORE and Rule.model.MAX_RULE_SCORE.

        Condition implemented by the rule.
    variables : str

    duplicate_rule(rule: str | [Rule](../rule/#ds_api.rule.Rule "            Rule \(ds_api.rule.Rule\)"), find_by: str = [OBJECT](../utils/#ds_api.utils.ResourceSearchTypeEnum.OBJECT "            OBJECT

Duplicate the given rule within this project.

Optionally, you can change some attributes of the duplicate.

If an object is provided to the `rule` parameter, the rule doesn't have to belong to this project. It will take the properties of the given rule, and create a new one within this project with those properties.

####  `find_by` Â¶

  * 'id' : duplicate rule with the given id.
  * 'desc' : duplicate rule with the given user visible name.
  * (Default) 'object' : duplicate the given rule.

|  `'id'`  
  
####  `new_desc` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the duplicated rule. Defaults to the original name suffixed with '_duplicate'. |  `None`  
  
####  `new_control_flow` Â¶

|  `[ControlFlowActionTypeEnum](../rule/#ds_api.rule.ControlFlowActionTypeEnum "            ControlFlowActionTypeEnum \(ds_api.rule.ControlFlowActionTypeEnum\)") | None` |  The control flow to use in the duplicate. Defaults to the original. |  `None`  
  
####  `new_actions` Â¶

####  `new_comment` Â¶

|  `str` |  The comment to use in the duplicate. Defaults to the original. |  `None`  
  
####  `new_variables` Â¶

|  `str` |  The variables to use in the duplicate. Defaults to the original. |  `None`  
  
####  `new_condition` Â¶

|  `str` |  The condition to use in the duplicate. Defaults to the original. |  `None`  
  
####  `reload` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If the rules project should be reloaded to reflect the added rule, by default True. This could be used when creating multiple rules, to avoid reloading on every creation. By default True. |  `True`

###  `` shift_rule Â¶
    
    
    shift_rule(rule: str, find_by: str, offset: int) -> None
    

Moves the rule the number of positions specified by the offset.

This will in shift this rule in the rules project's ordered list of rules, copying the rule order value between affected rules. Therefore, even if the order values are not continuous (e.g. 1, 3, 5) they will be preserved as a whole but assigned to different rules (i.e. after any shift the order values will still be 1, 3 and 5).

A rule will only be shifted forward (positive offset) or backwards (negative offset) at most until it reaches the end or beginning of the list, respectively - i.e. the list is not treated like a circular buffer.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `rule` Â¶

|  `str` |  The value of the rule property, specified by `find_by`, which identifies the rule to shift. |  _required_  
  
####  `find_by` Â¶

|  `('id', 'desc')` |  Name of the property of rules, which uniquely identifies the rule to shift, whose value is provided by the `rule`:

  * 'id' : shift rule with the given id.
  * 'desc' : shift rule with the given user visible name.

|  `'id'`  
  
####  `offset` Â¶

|  `int` |  The number of positions to move the rule. It can be positive to move the rule `offset` positions forward or negative to move it backwards. |  _required_  
  
###  `` to_dict Â¶
    
    
    to_dict(include_params: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]
    

Get a dictionary representation of this project and its rules.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `include_params` Â¶

|  `Sequence[str]` |  Properties of the rules project to include in the dictionary. Can be one of {'id', 'desc', 'app_id', 'tenancy_type', 'schema_id', 'target_field', 'actions', 'rules'} |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict[str, Any]` |  A DataFrame with the properties for all rules in the project, along with the project's properties.  
  
###  `` update_rule Â¶
    
    
    update_rule(rule: str, find_by: str, reload: bool = True, **kwargs: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None
    

Update several properties of a rule associated with this project.

Warning

This method doesn't check whether the properties you pass as a kwarg is one of the existing modifiable ones, so it's up to the caller to make sure the keys are correct.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `rule` Â¶

|  `str` |  The value of the rule property, specified by `find_by`, which identifies the rule to update. |  _required_  
  
####  `find_by` Â¶

|  `('id', 'desc')` |  Name of the property of a Rule, which uniquely identifies the rule to update, which describes the given `rule`:

  * 'id' : update rule with the given id.
  * 'desc' : update rule with the given user visible name.

|  `'id'`  
  
####  `reload` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If the rules project should be reloaded to reflect the added rule, by default True. This could be used when creating multiple rules, to avoid reloading on every creation. By default True. |  `True`  
  
####  `**kwargs` Â¶

|  `Mapping[str, Any]` |  Map between rule property names and the values we want to update them to |  `{}`  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`TypeError` |  If any of the given property values aren't of the required type  
  
##  `` RulesProjectTenancyTypeEnum Â¶

Bases: `[JavaEnumWrapper](../java_enum_wrapper/#ds_api.java_enum_wrapper.JavaEnumWrapper "            JavaEnumWrapper \(ds_api.java_enum_wrapper.JavaEnumWrapper\)")`

Possible types of tenancy for rules projects.

###  `` MULTI_TENANT_DISTINCT `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MULTI_TENANT_DISTINCT = auto()
    

Multi-tenant distinct. Each tenant may have its individual rules.

###  `` MULTI_TENANT_SHARED `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MULTI_TENANT_SHARED = auto()
    

Multi-tenant shared. Rules are common to all tenants but are processed independently by each tenant. This means events are filtered by tenant on evaluations, and that on stateful rules each tenant has a dedicated rules engine.

###  `` SINGLE_TENANT `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    SINGLE_TENANT = auto()
    

Single tenant, the default. There is no tenant segregation.

Title: Index - DS API
============================================================

#  `` sampling Â¶  
  
Efficient sampling utilities through PySpark. This sub-package provides a stratified and a random sampling strategy, but can be easily extended to user defined strategies.

It supports smart optimized sampling that preserves the minimal history of entities, so historical profiles can be correctly computed while maximizing the sampling reduction power.

If you intend to implement your own sampling algorithm, that's fine: there's a [`BaseSampler`](base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.BaseSampler "            BaseSampler") that takes care of the integration with Pulse through the ds-api.

###  `` add_field Â¶
    
    
    add_field(field: [FieldTemplate](../field/#ds_api.field.FieldTemplate "            FieldTemplate
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.field.FieldTemplate\)"), index: int | None = None)
    

Adds a new field to the schema.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `field` Â¶

|  `[FieldTemplate](../field/#ds_api.field.FieldTemplate "            FieldTemplate


  
      dataclass
   \(ds_api.field.FieldTemplate\)")` |  The new field, which is created according to the field template and added to the schema. |  _required_  
  
####  `index` Â¶

|  `int` |  The index of the new field in the Schema's list of fields. For example, if `index = 0`, the new schema will be added in the first position. If no index is specified, the field is appended to the end of the Schema. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> schema = app.pullSchemaByDesc(name="target-schema")
    >>> schema.add_field(field=FieldTemplate(
    ...     "numeric_field",
    ...     NativeType.FLOAT,
    ...     NumericalField())
    ... )
    >>> schema.add_field(field=
    ...     FieldTemplate("categorical_field",
    ...         NativeType.STRING,
    ...         CategoricalField({"value1", "value2"})
    ... ), index=0)
    

###  `` add_fields Â¶
    
    
    add_fields(fields: list[[FieldTemplate](../field/#ds_api.field.FieldTemplate "            FieldTemplate
    
    
      
          dataclass
       \(ds_api.field.FieldTemplate\)")])
    

Adds a list of new fields to the schema. The fields are added at the end of the schema following the order of the provided list.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `fields` Â¶

|  `list of FieldTemplate` |  The list of new fields, which are created according to the corresponding field template and added to the schema. |  _required_  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> schema = app.pullSchemaByDesc(name="target-schema")
    >>> fields = [
    ...     FieldTemplate("numeric_field", NativeType.FLOAT, NumericalField()),
    ...     FieldTemplate(
    ...         "timestamp_msec_field",
    ...         NativeType.LONG,
    ...         DateTimeField(TimestampFormat("MILLISECONDS"))
    ...     ),
    ... ]
    >>> schema.add_fields(fields)

###  `` reorder_fields Â¶
    
    
    reorder_fields(order: tuple[int, ...])
    

Reorders the fields in the schema according to the specified list of indices. The new order is specified with a tuple of integers where each element is the index of the field in the original list of fields.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `order` Â¶

|  `tuple of int` |  Tuple with the field indices in the new order. |  _required_  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`(ValueError, TypeError)` |  If the specified order is not valid.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> schema = app.pullSchemaByDesc(name="four-field-schema")
    >>> schema.reorder_fields((1, 0, 2, 3))
    >>> schema.reorder_fields((3, 2, 1, 0))
    

###  `` reorder_fields_by_name Â¶

Reorders the fields in the schema according to the specified list of names.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `ordered_names` Â¶

Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`(ValueError, TypeError)` |  If the specified list of names is not an exact match to the schema field names.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> app = Feedzai().app(name="sample")
    >>> schema = app.pullSchemaByDesc(name="four-field-schema")
    >>> schema.reorder_fields_by_name(("field2", "field1", "field3", "field4"))
    >>> schema.reorder_fields_by_name(("field4", "field3", "field2", "field1"))
    

##  `` get_internal_name Â¶
    
    
    get_internal_name(desc: str, schema: Schema | None = None) -> str
    

Return the internal (sanitized) name of a field.

This is a utility function to improve the UX of ds-api. Through ds_api the user should only care about the names as they appear in the UI ('desc').

Note

If a name is not found or the schema is not passed, this function acts as a no-op.

##  `` get_internal_names Â¶
    
    
    get_internal_names(names: _T, schema: Schema | None = None) -> _T
    

Return a sequence of internal (sanitized) names based on names as they appear in the UI.

Examples:
    
    
    >>> get_internal_names({"A Fancy Name here", "and another there"}, schema)
    {"a_fancy_name_here", "and_another_there"}
    

See Also

`ds_api.schema.get_internal_name`
