##  `` SparkMasterURL `dataclass` Â¶
    
    
    SparkMasterURL(master: str, deploy_mode: str | None)
    

Stores information about the Spark master URL.

This class is useful to parse master URLs from Pulse Configuration, while maintaining compatibility with different Spark versions/distributions.

See more at [Submitting Applications / Master URLs](https://spark.apache.org/docs/3.3.1/submitting-applications.html#master-urls)

Attributes:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`master` |  `str` |  Spark master URL. One of 'local', 'spark', 'mesos', 'yarn', or 'k8s'.  
`deploy_mode` |  `(str, optional)` |  Deploy mode for the different master URLs. One of 'client' or 'cluster'.  
  
###  `` deploy_mode `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    deploy_mode: str | None
    

###  `` master `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    master: str
    

###  `` from_pulse_config `classmethod` Â¶
    
    
    from_pulse_config(config: Any) -> SparkMasterURL
    

Return the master URL compatible with Spark 2 onwards.

Previous URLs would be called local, yarn-client, yarn-cluster. However, Spark 2 introduced a "special" Spark master which is just YARN and delegates the deployment mode to Hadoop. The new options are::
    
    
    --master yarn --deploy-mode client|cluster
    

This function translates 'yarn-client' and 'yarn-cluster' to their respective Spark 2+ configs.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `config` Â¶

|  `SparkConfig` |  Spark Config as returned by :func:`get_spark_session_with_app_name` or :func:`get_spark_session_without_app_name`. |  _required_  
  
##  `` add_kerberos_to_spark_properties Â¶
    
    
    add_kerberos_to_spark_properties(pulse: [Server](../server/#ds_api.server.Server "            Server \(ds_api.server.Server\)"), spark_config: dict) -> None
    

Adds kerberos configuration to `spark_config`.

Note

This function mutates the contents of `spark_config`.

##  `` get_spark_config_with_app_name Â¶
    
    
    get_spark_config_with_app_name(pulse: [Server](../server/#ds_api.server.Server "            Server \(ds_api.server.Server\)"), app_name: str | None, job_profile_name: str | None = None, group_name: str | None = None, clean_spark_properties: bool = True) -> Any
    

Retrieves the effective 'com.feedzai.pulse.service.datascience.config.SparkConfig' from Pulse Server to be used by an external Spark job. These configurations will depend on a given optional job profile and group model.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `pulse` Â¶

|  `[Server](../server/#ds_api.server.Server "            Server \(ds_api.Server\)")` |  The ds_api.Server used to communicate with Pulse. |  _required_  
  
###  `app_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  An optional name of a pulse application. This parameter should be used when we want to have access to application specific properties. This allows to use use the same properties being usd by Spark jobs executed in Pulse. |  _required_  
  
###  `job_profile_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the job profile to reuse the Spark properties defined there. If 'None' then it will retrieve the properties associated to default job profile for jobs of type 'EXTERNAL_DS_JOB'. |  `None`  
  
###  `group_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the group model used to retrieve the configurations defined in its properties. |  `None`  
  
###  `clean_spark_properties` Â¶

|  `bool` |  True if it should clean the Spark properties specific to Pulse use case, false otherwise. |  `True`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`SparkConfig` |  A com.feedzai.pulse.service.datascience.config.SparkConfig with Pulse's configuration.  
  
##  `` get_spark_config_without_app_name Â¶
    
    
    get_spark_config_without_app_name(pulse: [Server](../server/#ds_api.server.Server "            Server \(ds_api.server.Server\)")) -> Any
    

Retrieves the effective 'com.feedzai.pulse.service.datascience.config.SparkConfig' from configurations defined in the global scope of 'com.feedzai.pulse.service.datascience.config.DataScienceConfig' and `com.feedzai.pulse.service.distributed.job.config.DistributedJobServiceConfig`.

The DataScienceConfig has priority over DistributedJobServiceConfig.

---|---  
`unknown` |  A 'com.feedzai.pulse.service.datascience.config.SparkConfig' from configurations defined in the global scope of data science service configuration.

##  `` cast_java_subclass Â¶
    
    
    cast_java_subclass(java_object)
    

Helper method that casts a java class to its real subclass implementation.

For context, for example, imagine the class Format that has as subclasses DateTimeFormat and TimestampFormat.

When java returns an object with type Format and we know it is a TimestampFormat, we first need to cast it to this subclass so we can for example access its getUnit() specific method.

##  `` to_serializable Â¶
    
    
    to_serializable(obj: Any) -> Any
    

Convert int, float, str to their serializable Java version:

  * `int` -> `java.lang.Long`
  * `float` -> `java.lang.Double`
  * `str` -> `java.lang.String`
  * None -> None



##  `` transform_java_object Â¶
    
    
    transform_java_object(java_object)
    

Transforms a java object into the correspondent python object.

  1. If the provided object is already a python object, then it will simply be returned without any change. A java object is defined as an object that implements jnius.JavaClass.
  2. If the provided object matches any of the specific transformations in transformations, then the correspondent custom transformation is applied.
  3. If none of the above rules apply, then the object will be wrapped in a JavaWrapper object.



Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `java_object` Â¶

|  |  The java object to be transformed. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Any` |  The correspondent python object.

##  `` camel_to_snake Â¶
    
    
    camel_to_snake(string: str) -> str
    

Convert camel case to snake case.

Excludes strings starting with underscore, or in upper case eg: ["createObj", "create_obj", "**dict** ", "CONSTANT"] = ['create_obj', 'create_obj', '**dict** ', 'CONSTANT']

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default

|  `str` |  input string |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---

##  `` create_spark_app_name Â¶
    
    
    create_spark_app_name() -> str
    

Creates an application name for spark.

The created name will take in consideration the following aspects:

  1. If the env variable ds_api.USER_NAME_ENV is defined, then it will be used as part of the created name. This environment variable is assumed to contain the name of the user that spawned the current process.
  2. If the env variable in 1) is not defined, then the env variable ds_api.USER_ID_ENV will be used (if defined) as part of the created name. This environment variable is assumed to contain the identifier of the user that spawned the current process.
  3. If the env variable in 2) is not defined, then this function calls os.getpid, and uses such pid in the created application name.



Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`str` |  A spark application name.

`job_properties` |  `Dict` |  The dictionary with the specific properties for the schema inference job. Will only be used if no Schema is specified.  
`n_records` |  `int` |  The number of records that will be read per partition when performing datasource schema inference. Defaults to 15000. Will only be used if no Schema is specified.  
`dtypes` |  ` Dict` |  The dictionary with the field overriding information when inferring the datasource schema. Will only be used if no Schema is specified.  
`merge_schemas` |  `bool` |  Specify if schema merging should be allowed. See: https://spark.apache.org/docs/latest/sql-data-sources-parquet.html#schema-merging

##  `` SparkDatasourceAdapter Â¶
    
    
    SparkDatasourceAdapter(spark_session)
    

Bases: `object`

A class that follows the adapter pattern. This class is responsible for:

  * adapting pulse data sources into spark dataframes, through `from_pulse_datasource`
  * adapting spark dataframes into pulse data sources, through `to_pulse_datasource`

    from_pulse_datasource(app, name)
    

Adapts a pulse data source into a pyspark dataframe.

###  `` to_pulse_datasource Â¶
    
    
    to_pulse_datasource(dataframe: _TDataFrame, app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), name: str, path: str, file_format: str = 'csv', schema: Optional[[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")] = None, **kwargs: Union[bool, str, int, Dict[str, Any], Set[str]])
    

Adapts a pyspark dataframe into a pulse datasource. The dataframe will be written in the path specified before being added to Pulse. DateType and TimestampType fields are written as unix time (in milliseconds). The schema will be automatically inferred using AbstractDataSource#inferSchema approach if the Schema is not specified.

All pySpark types but DecimalType and MLlib types are supported.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `dataframe` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  DataFrame which will be used to create a Pulse datasource. |  _required_  
  
####  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)")` |  The App where the datasource will be created. |  _required_  
  
####  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the datasource. |  _required_  
  
####  `path` Â¶

|  `str` |  The path of the file containing the dataset. |  _required_  
  
####  `file_format` Â¶

|  `str` |  The input file format. Options are `csv` and `parquet`. Defaults to `csv`. |  `'csv'`  
  
####  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  The Pulse Schema to use. Optional. If not specified, the Schema will be inferred by Pulse. Only used for csv files. |  `None`  
  
####  `kwargs` Â¶

|  `Dict` |  Keyword arguments used for the configuration of the DataSource and for the SchemaInference job. |  `{}`  
  
Other Parameters:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`has_header` |  `bool` |  Flags whether the dataset is to be read with header. Only used for csv files. Defaults to True.  
`sep` |  `str` |  Delimiter to use. Should contain exactly one character. Only used for csv files. Defaults to ','.  
`missing_values` |  `set of str` |  Set of strings with values for empty value. Only used for csv files. Defaults to {"", "NA", "NULL"}.  
`quote` |  `str` |  The character used to denote the start and end of a quoted item. Only used for csv files. Defaults to "'".  
`line_separator` |  `str` |  The string used mark the end of line. Only used for csv files. Defaults to "\n".  
`encoding` |  `str` |  Encoding to use for UTF when reading/writing. Only used for csv files. Defaults to 'UTF-8'.  
`cat_threshold` |  `int` |  A value that represents the maximum number of distinct values on a given field for it to be inferred as categorical. Defaults to 15. Will only be used if no Schema is specified.  
`job_properties` |  `Dict` |  The dictionary with the specific properties for the schema inference job. Will only be used if no Schema is specified. Defaults to Pulse PropertiesProfile.  
`n_records` |  `int` |  The number of records that will be read per partition when performing datasource schema inference. Will only be used if no Schema is specified. Defaults to 15000.  
`dtypes` |  ` Dict` |  The dictionary with the field overriding information when inferring the datasource schema. Will only be used for csv files and if no Schema is specified. Defaults to None.  
`merge_schemas` |  `bool` |  Specify if schema merging should be allowed. Only used for parquet files. Defaults to False. See: https://spark.apache.org/docs/latest/sql-data-sources-parquet.html#schema-merging  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`[DataSource](../../datasource/#ds_api.datasource.DataSource "            DataSource \(ds_api.datasource.DataSource\)")` |  The new DataSource instance.

#  `` constants Â¶

A set of useful numerical, and categorical constants.

## HyperLogLog constantsÂ¶

We use $2^{12}$ registers for the HyperLogLog algorithm, equivalent to a cardinality error of 1.625%. The relative error can be estimated from

$$ \frac{1.04}{\sqrt{m}}, $$

with $m = 2^k$ the number of registers.

Attributes:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`HLL_K` |  `int` |  Number of registries for HyperLogLog.  
`HLL_ERROR_RATIO` |  `float` |  Cardinality relative error.  
  
##  `` AGGREGATION_MAPPER `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    AGGREGATION_MAPPER = {'min': BIN_MIN_COL, 'max': BIN_MAX_COL, 'count': COUNT_COL, 'avg': MEAN_COL, 'sum': SUM_COL}

#  `` exploration_results Â¶

##  `` SAMPLE_SIZE `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    SAMPLE_SIZE = 500
    

Sample size.

##  `` ExplorationResults Â¶
    
    
    ExplorationResults(results_dict: dict, max_bins: int, time_resolution: str, breakdowns: list, n_rows: int, timestamp_field: str | None = None, cost_field: str | None = None, target=(None, None), data: DataFrame | _TDataFrame | None = None, sample_size: int | None = 100000, report_html: str | None = None)
    

Bases: `object`

Results of the exploration compute_stats.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `results_dict` Â¶

|  `dict` |  dictionary-like results obtained from exploration.stats.compute_stats(). |  _required_  
  
###  `max_bins` Â¶

|  `int` |  binning resolution used for the histogram computation of numerical fields. In practice, this value defines the maximum number of bins that can be used to draw histograms for numerical fields. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  `str` |  time resolution used for the histogram computation of datetime fields. |  _required_  
  
###  `breakdowns` Â¶

|  `list` |  a list of field names that were used to calculate breakdowns over. |  _required_  
  
###  `n_rows` Â¶

|  `int` |  number of rows of the explored dataset. |  _required_  
  
###  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  name of the timestamp field. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `cost_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  name of the cost field. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `target` Â¶

|  |  tuple containing (target_field, target_value). Element target_field is the name of the target field. Element target_value indicate the value to be considered as 'positives'. Default: None. |  `(None, None)`  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  `DataFrame | _TDataFrame | None` |  Pandas or PySpark DataFrame. A small sample (500 rows) will be stored as a Pandas DataFrame. If a target exists then the sample will be balanced with respect to the target value. This is useful for the sample section of the html report and to create scatter plots. Depending on the specification of parameter `sample_size`, this data will be used as well to compute the time dependencies. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `sample_size` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  number of instances taken from `data` to use in the computation of time dependencies. If -1 is given then all instances will be used. If `None` then time dependencies won't be computed. Default: 100k. |  `100000`  
  
###  `report_html` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  an existing html report in string format. If None is passed it will generate it. It's mainly useful when creating an ExplorationResults from serialized results using the 'load' method. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
Attributes:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`field_names` |  |  List of field names.  
`constant_fields` |  |  List of constant fields.  
`n_unhealthy` |  |  Number of unhealthy values (missing, nan, infs, empty) summed over all fields.  
`target_rate` |  |  Rate of positives.  
  
###  `` breakdowns `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    breakdowns = breakdowns
    

###  `` constant_fields `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    constant_fields = list((name) for [field](../../field/#ds_api.field "            field \(ds_api.field\)") in (values()) if is_constant)
    

###  `` cost_field `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    cost_field = cost_field
    

###  `` field_names `instance-attribute` Â¶

#  `` feature_stats Â¶  
  
##  `` collect_value_counts Â¶
    
    
    collect_value_counts(data: _TDataFrame, string_fields: list[str], dist_sampling: float | None) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]
    

Compute sampled value counts for string fields via groupBy.

It identifies the frequency of unique values within specified string columns.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  The source DataFrame containing string columns to analyze. |  _required_  
  
###  `string_fields` Â¶

|  `list of str` |  The names of the columns for which to compute value distributions. |  _required_  
  
###  `dist_sampling` Â¶

|  `float` |  The sampling fraction (0.0 to 1.0). If None or 0.0, the function returns an empty dictionary immediately. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict of (str, dict of (str, int))` |  A mapping where each key is a field name, and the value is a dictionary containing unique strings and their respective occurrence counts.  
  
##  `` datetime_stat_exprs Â¶
    
    
    datetime_stat_exprs(field_name: str) -> list[tuple[str, str, Column]]
    

Generate Spark aggregate expressions that compute statistics for a given datetime column.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the datetime column to analyze. The name is automatically escaped using backticks for SQL safety. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`list of tuple of (str, str, pyspark.sql.Column)` |  A list of triples containing (field_name, stat_description, spark_expression). The statistics included are: \- count: Total number of non-null datetime entries. \- missing: Count of NULL values in the column. \- min: The earliest (minimum) timestamp in the dataset. \- max: The latest (maximum) timestamp in the dataset.

##  `` numerical_stat_exprs Â¶
    
    
    numerical_stat_exprs(field_name: str) -> list[tuple[str, str, Column]]
    

Generate Spark aggregate expressions that compute statistics for a given numerical column.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the numerical column to analyze. The name is automatically escaped using backticks for SQL safety. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`list of tuple of (str, str, pyspark.sql.Column)` |  A list of triples containing (field_name, stat_description, spark_expression). The statistics included are: \- sum: Sum of all finite values. \- min: Minimum finite value. \- max: Maximum finite value. \- nan: Count of NaN values. \- zeros: Count of values equal to 0. \- infinites: Count of positive and negative infinity values. \- missing: Count of null values. \- count: Count of finite values. \- mean: Arithmetic mean of finite values. \- var: Sample variance of finite values.  
  
##  `` string_stat_exprs Â¶
    
    
    string_stat_exprs(field_name: str) -> list[tuple[str, str, Column]]
    

Generate Spark aggregate expressions that compute statistics for a given string column.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the string column to analyze. The name is automatically escaped using backticks for SQL safety. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`list of tuple of (str, str, pyspark.sql.Column)` |  A list of triples containing (field_name, stat_description, spark_expression). The statistics included are: \- count: Count of values that are neither NULL nor empty strings. \- empty: Count of empty string ("") values. \- missing: Count of NULL values. \- hll_cardinality: An approximate count of distinct values using the HyperLogLog++ algorithm for memory efficiency.  
  
##  `` validation_exprs Â¶
    
    
    validation_exprs(field_name: str, validations: list[[Validation](../validations/#ds_api.exploration.validations.Validation "            Validation \(ds_api.exploration.validations.Validation\)")]) -> list[tuple[str, str, Column]]
    

Build Spark aggregate expressions for custom validations on a field..

Iterates over a provided set of validations and generates a SQL expression that encompasses all provided validations.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  The name of the column to be validated. The name is automatically escaped using backticks for SQL safety. |  _required_  
  
###  `validations` Â¶

|  `list of Validation` |  A list of Validation objects, each containing a description and a 'sql_builder' method that accepts a Column and returns a Spark aggregate expression. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`list of tuple of (str, str, pyspark.sql.Column)` |  A list of triples containing (field_name, validation_description, spark_expression). These are typically collected into a single data.agg() call alongside standard statistics.

###  `` plot Â¶
    
    
    plot(nbins=50, fix_step=False, normalize=None, breakdown_by=None, stack=False, logy=False, interactive=True, mini=False)
    

Altair chart of the field.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `nbins` Â¶

|  |  approximate number of bins for the desired histogram visualization. Default: 50 |  `50`  
  
####  `fix_step` Â¶

|  |  if True, constrain the bin width to be a multiple of the original bin width. This ensures that every visual bucket, except for the first and last ones, contain the same amount of original buckets. Otherwise, the binning is automatically decided by Vega (i.e., the binning is optimized to be more pleasing to the human eye). Fixing the step leads to having more control over the desired number of bins, while simultaneously removing undesired bin migration effects. However, it may end up with unpleasant visual bin ranges. If enough resolution was used to calculate the histograms, the mistakes resulting from not fixing the step are likely to be small enough to be safely ignored. Therefore, the option False is preferred. Default is False. |  `False`  
  
####  `normalize` Â¶

|  |  bool (or None) indicating if the histogram is to be normalized. If set as True, the histogram is normalized to unity, otherwise the y-axis represents the actual counts. If set as True and breakdown_by is not None, the histogram of every breakdown value will be normalized to unity. Default is None: this means that the histogram will be normalized if breakdown_by is not None, otherwise it will not be normalized. |  `None`  
  
####  `breakdown_by` Â¶

|  |  string specifying a field to breakdown histograms by. Default is None: no breakdown. |  `None`  
  
####  `stack` Â¶

|  |  whether to stack histograms or not. Default: None. Only applies to the case where breakdown_by is not None. |  `False`  
  
####  `logy` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to use a log10 scale for the y-axis (default: False). |  `False`  
  
####  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart (default: True). |  `True`  
  
####  `mini` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating if this is a mini-histogram. |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`An Altair chart.` |   
  
###  `` plot_fraud_rate Â¶
    
    
    plot_fraud_rate(target=(None, None), nbins=50, fix_step=False, bar=True, logy=False, interactive=True)
    

Altair fraud rate chart of the field.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `target` Â¶

|  |  tuple containing (target_field, target_value). Element target_field is the name of the target field. Element target_value indicate the value to be considered as 'positives'. The target_value will be used for the numerator of the fraud rate. Default: (None, None), meaning use the exploration_results.target_field/value if existing. |  `(None, None)`  
  
####  `nbins` Â¶

|  |  approximate number of bins for the desired histogram visualization. Default: 50. |  `50`  
  
####  `fix_step` Â¶

|  |  if True, constrain the bin width to be a multiple of the original bin width. This ensures that every visual bucket, except for the first and last ones, contain the same amount of original buckets. Otherwise, the binning is automatically decided by Vega (i.e., the binning is optimized to be more pleasing to the human eye). Fixing the step leads to having more control over the desired number of bins, while simultaneously removing undesired bin migration effects. However, it may end up with unpleasant visual bin ranges. If enough resolution was used to calculate the histograms, the mistakes resulting from not fixing the step are likely to be small enough to be safely ignored. Therefore, the option False is preferred. Default is False. |  `False`  
  
####  `bar` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to return a bar chart (True) or a line chart (False). Default: True. |  `True`  
  
####  `logy` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to use a log10 scale for the y-axis (default: False). |  `False`  
  
####  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart (default: True). |  `True`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`An Altair chart.` |

####  `time_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the field associated with the temporal dimension. If None, the special timestamp_field will be used, if available. Default: None. |  `None`

###  `` plot_temporal_aggregation Â¶
    
    
    plot_temporal_aggregation(aggregate, time_field=None, time_resolution='1D', show_points=False, interactive=True, zero_scale=False, logy=False)
    

Altair chart of the field aggregated with respect to time.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `aggregate` Â¶

|  |  aggregation function to be used. Can be any of constants.SUPPORTED_AGGREGATIONS. |  _required_  
  
####  `time_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the field associated with the temporal dimension. If None, the special timestamp_field will be used, if available. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
####  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  resolution of the time dimension. Available resolutions have to be compliant with the `Pandas.Grouper` `freq` parameter, more details `here <https://pandas.pydata.org/pandas- docs/stable/user_guide/timeseries.html#offset-aliases>`_. Note that 1) this resolution can not be lower than the intrinsic time resolution of the passed dataframe and 2) for optimal results this resolution should be a multiple of the intrinsic time resolution. The intrinsic resolution is typically specified during the call to `compute_stats`. Default: '1D'. |  `'1D'`  
  
####  `show_points` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to display the data points (default: False). |  `False`  
  
####  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart (default: True). |  `True`  
  
####  `zero_scale` Â¶

|  |  If true, ensures that a zero baseline value is included in the scale domain. (default: False). |  `False`  
  
####  `logy` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to use a log10 scale for the y-axis (default: False). |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`An Altair chart.` |

####  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  resolution of the time dimension. Available resolutions have to be compliant with the `Pandas.Grouper` `freq` parameter, more details `here <https://pandas.pydata.org/pandas- docs/stable/user_guide/timeseries.html#offset-aliases>`_. Note that 1) this resolution can not be lower than the intrinsic time resolution of the passed dataframe and 2) for optimal results this resolution should be a multiple of the intrinsic time resolution. ###  `` quantile Â¶
    
    
    quantile(q=(0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0))
    

Return approximate value at the given quantile(s). Values are computed from the histograms, therefore the associated uncertainty is half the bin width of the histogram.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---

Type | Description

###  `` plot Â¶
    
    
    plot(sort_by_freq=True, breakdown_by=None, cat_threshold=None, vertical=True, logy=False, interactive=True, mini=False)
    

Altair chart of the field.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default

####  `sort_by_freq` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to sort by frequency, i.e. categories with higher frequency first. Otherwise, sort alphabetically (default: True). |  `True`  
  
####  `breakdown_by` Â¶

|  |  str specifying field to breakdown histograms by. Default is None: no breakdown. |  `None`  
  
####  `cat_threshold` Â¶

|  |  number of categories to keep. In case the underlying cardinality is larger, keep the cat_threshold-1 most frequent categories and create a new category 'other' as the aggregation of the rest of categories. Default is None: keep all categories. Only works for string histograms. |  `None`  
  
####  `vertical` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to produce a vertical or a a horizontal bar plot. Vertical means categories in the x-axis, counts on the y-axis. Horizontal only possible with no breakdowns. Default: vertical. |  `True`  
  
####  `logy` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to use a log10 scale for the y-axis (default: False). |  `False`  
  
####  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart. Default: True. |  `True`

|  |  bool indicating if this is a mini-histogram. |  `False`

`An Altair chart.` |   
  
###  `` plot_fraud_rate Â¶
    
    
    plot_fraud_rate(target=(None, None), sort_by='fr', cat_threshold=None, vertical=True, logy=False, interactive=True)
    

Altair fraud rate chart of the field.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `target` Â¶

|  |  tuple containing (target_field, target_value). Element target_field is the name of the target field. Element target_value indicate the value to be considered as 'positives'. The target_value will be used for the numerator of the fraud rate. Default: (None, None), meaning use the exploration_results.target_field/value if existing. |  `(None, None)`  
  
####  `sort_by` Â¶

|  |  string indicating sorting type. Supported sorting are by frequency ('freq'), by fraud rate ('fr') and alphabetical ('alpha'). Default: 'fr'. |  `'fr'`

####  `vertical` Â¶

##  `` fmt Â¶
    
    
    fmt(name, value, exact=False)
    

Format for a (name, value).

If name starts with `'f_'` it'll be considered as a fraction to be shown as a percent.

##  `` fmt_amount Â¶
    
    
    fmt_amount(number, significant_digits=3, currency='$')

##  `` fmt_cardinality_severity Â¶

More than 90% is considered an alert, and a 0 is considered to be ignored.

#  `` histograms Â¶

##  `` ConstantFieldError Â¶

Bases: `Exception`

Raised when numerical field is constant.

##  `` NumericalHistogramConfig Â¶
    
    
    NumericalHistogramConfig(minv, maxv, max_bins=1000)
    

Bases: `object`

Configuration object for numerical histograms.

It requires three configurable values: minv/maxv the minimum and maximum histogram values, and max_bins the number of bins or resolution of the histogram.

The minv and maxv configurables are private accessible via get_minmax() and set_minmax(), while nbins is a property.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `minv` Â¶

|  |  minimum value of the histogram. Smaller values will be counted in the underflow bin. |  _required_  
  
###  `maxv` Â¶

|  |  maximum value of the histogram. The last bin is closed on the right side, i.e. entries with value == maxv will be counted in the last bin. Larger values will be counted in the overflow bin. |  _required_  
  
###  `max_bins` Â¶

|  |  resolution of the histogram, i.e. number of bins within minv and maxv. There will be two extra bins to account for underflow and overflow. |  `1000`  
  
###  `` max_bins `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    max_bins
    

###  `` get_minmax Â¶
    
    
    get_minmax()
    

###  `` set_minmax Â¶
    
    
    set_minmax(minv, maxv)
    

Setter function for minv and maxv. It raises exceptions if they are not finite numbers or if minv > maxv.

##  `` TimeHistogramConfig Â¶
    
    
    TimeHistogramConfig(time_resolution='h')
    

Bases: `object`

Configuration object for datetime histograms. It requires one configurable value: the time_resolution of the plot.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  time resolution of the histogram. |  `'h'`  
  
###  `` time_resolution `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    time_resolution
    

##  `` build_numerical_bin_expr Â¶
    
    
    build_numerical_bin_expr(field: StructField, stats: dict, max_bins: int) -> Tuple[str, Column, Callable] | None
    

Builds a bin expression for a numerical field.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`tuple of (str, Column, callable)` |  `(field_name, bin_expr, decode_fn)` where _bin_expr_ maps each value to a string-encoded bin index and _decode_fn_ converts the string back to the `(bin_range, breakdown_values)` key used in the histogram dict. If the field is constant, returns `None`.  
  
##  `` build_string_bin_expr Â¶
    
    
    build_string_bin_expr(field: StructField, stats: dict, cardinality_threshold: int | None, dist_sampling: float | None) -> Tuple[str, Column, Callable]
    

Builds a bin expression for a string field.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`tuple of (str, Column, callable)` |  `(field_name, bin_expr, decode_fn)`.  
  
##  `` build_timestamp_bin_expr Â¶
    
    
    build_timestamp_bin_expr(field: StructField, time_config: TimeHistogramConfig) -> Tuple[str, Column, Callable]
    

Builds a bin expression for a timestamp field.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`tuple of (str, Column, callable)` |  `(field_name, bin_expr, decode_fn)`.  
  
##  `` compute_all_histograms Â¶
    
    
    compute_all_histograms(data: _TDataFrame, field_bin_exprs: list[Tuple[str, Column, Callable]], breakdown_cols: list[Column], breakdowns: list[str]) -> dict[str, dict]
    

Compute histograms for all fields in a single pass:

  1. Builds an array-of-structs with one element per field, each containing `(field_name_literal, bin_expression)`.
  2. Explodes the array so every input row produces N rows (one per field).
  3. Runs a single `groupBy("__field", "__bin", *breakdowns).count()`.
  4. Partitions the collected rows back into per-field histogram dicts using the field-specific _decode_fn_.



Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  Source DataFrame. |  _required_  
  
###  `field_bin_exprs` Â¶

|  `list of (str, Column, callable)` |  One tuple per field, as returned by `build_*_bin_expr`. Each tuple is `(field_name, bin_column_expr, decode_fn)`. |  _required_  
  
###  `breakdown_cols` Â¶

|  `list of Column` |  Pre-built breakdown column expressions for the `groupBy`. |  _required_  
  
###  `breakdowns` Â¶

|  `list of str` |  Field names corresponding to _breakdown_cols_. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict[str, dict]` |  Mapping from field name to histogram dict. Each histogram dict maps `(bucket_key, breakdown_values) -> count`.

##  `` plot_calendar Â¶
    
    
    plot_calendar(field_name, histogram_df, xaxis='date', yaxis='yearmonth', color='fr', size='freq', color_scheme=None, target_field=None, target_value=None)
    

Function to plot a calendar-like plot of a time variable using Altair.

Recommended combinations of axis units are (xaxis="date", yaxis="yearmonth") and (xaxis="hours", yaxis="yearmonthdate").

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  |  field name. |  _required_

|  |  time unit of the yaxis. Default: "yearmonthdate". |  `'yearmonth'`  
  
###  `color` Â¶

|  |  color encoding. It can be either 'freq' (for frequency counts) or 'fr' (for fraud rate). If set to 'fr' then parameters (target_field, target_value) must be given. |  `'fr'`  
  
###  `size` Â¶

|  |  size encoding. It can be either 'freq' (for frequency counts) or 'fr' (for fraud rate). If set to 'fr' then parameters (target_field, target_value) must be given. |  `'freq'`  
  
###  `color_scheme` Â¶

|  |  Vega color scheme to be used. Available schemes can be found here: https://vega.github.io/vega/docs/schemes/. Default is None, meaning 'blues' for 'freq' and diverging 'blueorange' centered at the overall fraud rate for 'fr'. |  `None`  
  
###  `target_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the target field. Must be given if color/size encoding contain 'fr'. Otherwise the given fraud rate encoding will be disabled. |  `None`  
  
###  `target_value` Â¶

|  |  value to be considered as 'positives' labels. Must be given if color/size encoding contain 'fr'. Otherwise the given fraud rate encoding will be disabled. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`unknown` |  an Altair chart.  
  
##  `` plot_fraud_rate_numerical Â¶
    
    
    plot_fraud_rate_numerical(field_name, histogram_df, target_field, target_value, nbins=50, fix_step=False, bar=True, logy=False, interactive=True)
    

Function for default fraud rate plotting of numerical variables using Altair.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  |  field name. |  _required_  
  
###  `histogram_df` Â¶

|  |  Pandas DataFrame representation of a histogram. |  _required_  
  
###  `target_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the target field. |  _required_  
  
###  `target_value` Â¶

|  |  value to be considered as 'positives' labels. |  _required_  
  
###  `nbins` Â¶

|  |  approximate number of bins for the desired histogram visualization. Default: 50 |  `50`  
  
###  `fix_step` Â¶

|  |  if True, constrain the bin width to be a multiple of the original bin width. This ensures that every visual bucket, except for the first and last ones, contain the same amount of original buckets. Otherwise, the binning is automatically decided by Vega (i.e., the binning is optimized to be more pleasing to the human eye). Fixing the step leads to having more control over the desired number of bins, while simultaneously removing undesired bin migration effects. However, it may end up with unpleasant visual bin ranges. If enough resolution was used to calculate the histograms, the mistakes resulting from not fixing the step are likely to be small enough to be safely ignored. Therefore, the option False is preferred. Default is False. |  `False`  
  
###  `bar` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to return a bar chart (True) or a line chart (False). Default: True. |  `True`  
  
###  `logy` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to use a log10 scale for the y-axis (default: False). |  `False`  
  
###  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart (default: True). |  `True`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`unknown` |  an Altair chart. If bar=False, this will be a layered Altair chart.

##  `` plot_numerical Â¶
    
    
    plot_numerical(field_name, histogram_df, nbins=50, fix_step=False, normalize=None, breakdown_by=None, stack=False, logy=False, interactive=True, mini=False)

###  `nbins` Â¶

|  |  approximate number of bins for the desired histogram visualization. Default: 50 |  `50`  
  
###  `fix_step` Â¶

|  |  if True, constrain the bin width to be a multiple of the original bin width. This ensures that every visual bucket, except for the first and last ones, contain the same amount of original buckets. Otherwise, the binning is automatically decided by Vega (i.e., the binning is optimized to be more pleasing to the human eye). Fixing the step leads to having more control over the desired number of bins, while simultaneously removing undesired bin migration effects. However, it may end up with unpleasant visual bin ranges. If enough resolution was used to calculate the histograms, the mistakes resulting from not fixing the step are likely to be small enough to be safely ignored. Therefore, the option False is preferred. Default is False. |  `False`  
  
###  `normalize` Â¶

|  |  bool (or None) indicating if the histogram is to be normalized. If set as True, the histogram is normalized to unity, otherwise the y-axis represents the actual counts. If set as True and breakdown_by is not None, the histogram of every breakdown value will be normalized to unity. Default is None: this means that the histogram will be normalized if breakdown_by is not None, otherwise it will not be normalized. |  `None`  
  
###  `breakdown_by` Â¶

###  `logy` Â¶

###  `interactive` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating whether to call the function interactive() on the Altair chart (default: True). |  `True`  
  
###  `mini` Â¶

|  |  bool indicating if this is a mini-histogram. |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`unknown` |  An Altair chart.  
  
##  `` plot_string Â¶
    
    
    plot_string(field_name, histogram_df, sort_by_freq=True, breakdown_by=None, cat_threshold=None, vertical=True, logy=False, interactive=True, mini=False)
    

Function for default plotting of categorical/string variables using Altair.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---

###  `vertical` Â¶

###  `logy` Â¶

##  `` compute_histograms Â¶
    
    
    compute_histograms(data, stats, breakdowns, cardinality_threshold, dist_sampling, max_bins, time_resolution)
    

Computes histograms.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  |  the Spark Dataframe with the data to be used in order to compute histograms. |  _required_  
  
###  `stats` Â¶

|  |  dictionary with statistics per field relevant for the computation of histograms. |  _required_  
  
###  `breakdowns` Â¶

|  |  a list of field names to calculate breakdowns over. |  _required_  
  
###  `cardinality_threshold` Â¶

|  |  cardinality threshold to decide if the full value counts is to be computed for string histograms. If the estimated cardinality is smaller than this value, then the full string histogram computation will be performed. Otherwise, only the top most frequent values are to be counted. |  _required_  
  
###  `dist_sampling` Â¶

|  |  sampling factor between 0 and 1 used for the estimation of value_counts for string fields during the compute_stats phase. |  _required_  
  
###  `max_bins` Â¶

|  |  binning resolution for the histogram computation of numerical fields. This value define the number of uniform buckets to be computed between to minimum and the maximum value of the field. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  time resolution for the histogram computation of datetime fields. Supported resolutions: 'h' for hours, 'd' for days. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict` |  a dictionary with the descriptive statistics.  
  
##  `` compute_stats Â¶
    
    
    compute_stats(data: _TDataFrame, semantics_map: dict | None = None, validations: Any = None, timestamp_field: str | None = None, cost_field: str | None = None, target: tuple | None = None, breakdowns: list[str] | None = None, breakdown_card_threshold: int = 10, cardinality_threshold: int | None = None, cardinality_sample_size: int = 100000, max_bins: int = 1000, time_resolution: str = 'h', time_dependencies_sample_size: int = 100000, checkpoint_file: str = CHECKPOINT_FILE)
    

Computes the descriptive statistics and optional validations for all the fields contained in the data. The statistics/validations will be returned in a dictionary whose keys are the fields in the dataset and the values will be dictionaries with the descriptive statistics/validations for those fields.

The computation will be performed over the RDD representation of the dataset in a single pass over the data through a map and a reduce step. The map operation will be applied to an entire row and will result in a dictionary with the mapped values for each feature and statistic/validation per row. Then a single reduce operation will be applied, reducing the dataset to a single dictionary with the aggregated statistics/validations.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  `_TDataFrame` |  the Spark Dataframe with the data to be used in order to compute statistics. |  _required_  
  
###  `semantics_map` Â¶

|  `dict | None` |  dictionary matching fields (keys) to semantic tags (values). Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `validations` Â¶

|  `Any` |  list/tuple/set containing list of Validation. It will be appended to the built-in set of validations. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  name of the column to be associated as timestamp field. Will be tagged with the TIMESTAMP semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `cost_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  name of the column to be associated as cost field. Will be tagged with the AMOUNT semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `target` Â¶

|  `tuple | None` |  tuple containing (target_field, target_value). Target field is the name of the columns to be associated as the target field, will be included in the list of breakdowns. Will be tagged with the AMOUNT semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Target value indicate the value to be considered as 'positives'. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `breakdowns` Â¶

|  `list[str] | None` |  a list of field names to calculate breakdowns over. So far breakdowns are only relevant for histograms. |  `None`  
  
###  `breakdown_card_threshold` Â¶

|  `int` |  cardinality threshold for breakdown fields. A protection is added so if a specified breakdown field has a cardinality larger than this threshold, then it will be removed from the list of fields to calculate breakdowns over. Default value is 10. |  `10`  
  
###  `cardinality_threshold` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  cardinality threshold to decide if the full value counts is to be computed for string histograms. If the estimated cardinality is smaller than this value, then the full string histogram computation will be performed. Otherwise, only the top most frequent values are to be counted. Default is None, meaning that the full computation will be performed. |  `None`  
  
###  `cardinality_sample_size` Â¶

|  `int` |  sample size used for the estimation of value_counts for string fields. If a negative value is given then the full sample will be used (not recommended for samples larger than 1M instances). If None is given, then no value_counts computation will be performed. Default is 100k. |  `100000`  
  
###  `max_bins` Â¶

|  `int` |  binning resolution for the histogram computation of numerical fields. This value defines the number of uniform buckets to be computed between the minimum and the maximum value of the field. Default is 1000. |  `1000`  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  `str` |  time resolution for the histogram computation of datetime fields. Supported resolutions: 'h' for hours, 'd' for days. Default is 'd'. |  `'h'`  
  
###  `time_dependencies_sample_size` Â¶

|  `int` |  number of instances to use in the computation of time dependencies. If a negative value is given then all instances will be used. If `None` is given then time dependencies won't be computed. Default: 100k. |  `100000`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`unknown` |  an instance of ExplorationResults containing the results of the computed statistics, validations and histograms.

    compute_stats_from_checkpoint(checkpoint_file=CHECKPOINT_FILE, data=None)

##  `` run_validations Â¶
    
    
    run_validations(data, semantics_map, validations=None, timestamp_field=None, cost_field=None, target=None)
    

Computes the specified validations per field contained in the data. The validations will be returned in a dictionary whose keys are the fields in the dataset and the values will be dictionaries with the validations for those fields.

The computation will be performed over the RDD representation of the dataset in a single pass over the data through a map and a reduce step. The map operation will be applied to an entire row and will result in a dictionary with the mapped values for each feature and validation per row. Then a single reduce operation will be applied, reducing the dataset to a single dictionary with the aggregated validations.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  |  the Spark Dataframe with the data to be used in order to compute statistics. |  _required_  
  
###  `semantics_map` Â¶

|  |  dictionary matching fields (keys) to semantic tags (values). |  _required_  
  
###  `validations` Â¶

|  |  list/tuple/set containing list of Validation. It will be appended to the built-in set of validations. |  `None`  
  
###  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the column to be associated as timestamp field. Will be tagged with the TIMESTAMP semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `cost_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the column to be associated as cost field. Will be tagged with the AMOUNT semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
###  `target` Â¶

|  |  tuple containing (target_field, target_value). Target field is the name of the columns to be associated as the target field. Will be tagged with the TARGET semantic and associated built-in validations will be performed. Target value indicate the value to be considered as 'positives'. Default: None. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict` |  a dictionary with the descriptive validations.

#  `` time_dependencies Â¶

##  `` LOGGER `module-attribute` Â¶

##  `` compute_time_dependencies Â¶
    
    
    compute_time_dependencies(df, time_field, sample_size=100000, missing_value_strategy='impute')
    

Computes a list of fields ordered by their dependency with time. To obtain the degree of dependency, a model is trained to predict the timestamp field. The dependency score of each feature is its feature importance on that regression model.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `df` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  a PySpark DataFrame. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  name of the reference time field over which the dependencies are calculated. |  _required_  
  
###  `sample_size` Â¶

|  `int` |  Number of instances to keep after sampling. The sampled dataset will be fed to the model. This is needed in order for the computation to run in a feasible time, for large datasets. If the dataframe is smaller than sample_size, then all instances are used. Default is 100k. |  `100000`  
  
###  `missing_value_strategy` Â¶

|  `str` |  method to use for missing value treatment. Possible values are "impute" and "drop". "Impute" fills missing values with the mean, for numerical and datetime fields, and with the constant values "(missing)", for string fields. "Impute" may cause the dependency to be inflated if the missing values of a field are concentrated in the same region of time. Default is "impute". |  `'impute'`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Series` |  Pandas Series where each row contains the name of a feature (as the index) and a value representing its dependency with time. Sorted in descending order. The higher the value, the stronger the dependency.

##  `` aggregate_function_over_time Â¶
    
    
    aggregate_function_over_time(df, time_field, field_name, aggregate='avg', time_resolution='1 day')
    

Function to process a PySpark DataFrame, computing a given aggregate function on a given field over time windows defined by a time resolution.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `df` Â¶

|  |  PySpark DataFrame. It should contain at least two columns, one with the value of time_field as column name and another with the value of field_name. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the time field. |  _required_  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the field used to calculate the aggregation over. |  _required_  
  
###  `aggregate` Â¶

|  |  aggregation function to be used. The available functions are "avg", "max", "min", "sum", "count". UDF created with pyspark.sql.functions.pandas_udf() may also be used. See https:/ /spark.apache.org/docs/latest/api/python/pyspark.sql.html#pyspar k.sql.GroupedData for more details. (default: avg). |  `'avg'`  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  time resolution to be used is provided as a string containing a number and an interval or a combination of those. For example, '1 day 12 hours'. Valid intervals are 'week', 'day', 'hour', 'minute', 'second', 'millisecond', 'microsecond'. All valid values for the windowDuration parameter of pyspark.sql.functions.window are accepted. See http://spark.apac he.org/docs/latest/api/python/pyspark.sql.html?highlight=window# module-pyspark.sql.functions for more details. (default: 1 day). |  `'1 day'`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  Pandas DataFrame with two columns. One is the time field column, it has the value of time_field as the column name and contains the start datetime for each time window. The second column contains the values of the aggregation function and its name is the name of the aggregation function that was used.  
  
##  `` count_top_n_over_time Â¶
    
    
    count_top_n_over_time(df, time_field, field_name, top_n=5, time_resolution='1 day')
    

Function to process a PySpark DataFrame, computing the counts of the top n most frequent categories of a given string field over a time windows defined by a time resolution.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `df` Â¶

|  |  Pyspark DataFrame. It should contain at least two columns, one with the value of time_field as column name and another with the value of field_name. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the time field. |  _required_  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the string field used to calculate the top N values aggregation over time. |  _required_  
  
###  `top_n` Â¶

|  |  the number of the top most frequent categorical values to be drawn. |  `5`  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  time resolution to be used is provided as a string containing a number and an interval or a combination of those. For example, '1 day 12 hours'. Valid intervals are 'week', 'day', 'hour', 'minute', 'second', 'millisecond', 'microsecond'. All valid values for the windowDuration parameter of pyspark.sql.functions.window are accepted. See http://spark.apac he.org/docs/latest/api/python/pyspark.sql.html?highlight=window# module-pyspark.sql.functions for more details. (default: 1 day). |  `'1 day'`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  Pandas Dataframe with three columns. One is the time field column, it has the value of time_field as the column name and contains the start datetime for each time window. The second contains the frequency counts of the top n values of the string field, over that time window, and is called "count". Lastly, the third contains the name of that categorical value, and the column name is the value of field_name.

##  `` histogram2df_fraud_rate Â¶
    
    
    histogram2df_fraud_rate(histogram_df, field_type, target_field, target_value, cat_threshold=None, time_resolution=None)
    

Transforms the given histogram into a convenient Pandas DataFrame including a fraud rate column.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `histogram_df` Â¶

|  |  Pandas DataFrame representation of a histogram. |  _required_  
  
###  `target_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the target field. |  _required_  
  
###  `target_value` Â¶

|  |  value to be considered as 'positives' labels. |  _required_  
  
###  `cat_threshold` Â¶

|  |  number of categories to keep. In case the underlying cardinality is larger, keep the cat_threshold most frequent categories and create a new category 'other' as the aggregation of the rest of categories. The 'missing' category is always treated as a special category, meaning that it will not be merged into 'other' and it will be ignored when finding the cat_threshold most frequent categories. Default is None: keep all categories. Only relevant for string histograms. |  `None`  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  project time field to given time_resolution. Default is None: keep underlying time resolution. Only relevant for time histograms. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  pandas DataFrame representation of the histogram including a fraud rate column.

##  `` quantiles_over_time Â¶
    
    
    quantiles_over_time(df, time_field, field_name, time_resolution='1 day')
    

Function to process a PySpark DataFrame, computing the min, max and quantiles of a given field over time windows defined by a time resolution. The quantiles are approximated, yielding a relative error of 0.01%. See https://spark.apache.org/docs/2.3.0/api/sql/index.html#percentile_approx for more details.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `df` Â¶

|  |  PySpark DataFrame. It should contain at least two columns, one with the value of time_field as column name and another with the value of field_name. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_field` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the time field. |  _required_  
  
###  `field_name` Â¶

|  |  name of the PySpark DataFrame column associated to the field used to calculate the quantiles over. |  _required_  
  
###  `time_resolution` Â¶

|  |  time resolution to be used is provided as a string containing a number and an interval or a combination of those. For example, '1 day 12 hours'. Valid intervals are 'week', 'day', 'hour', 'minute', 'second', 'millisecond', 'microsecond'. All valid values for the windowDuration parameter of pyspark.sql.functions.window are accepted. See http://spark.apac he.org/docs/latest/api/python/pyspark.sql.html?highlight=window# module-pyspark.sql.functions for more details. (default: 1 day). |  `'1 day'`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  Pandas DataFrame with six columns. One is the time field column, it has the value of time_field as the column name and contains the start datetime for each time window. The others contain the values of the min, max and the 25%, 50% and 75% quantiles. They are called min, 25%, median, 75% and max and appear in this order.

Other Parameters:

`breakdown_by` |  `str` |  field to breakdown by. If None (default) then don't breakdown. If the original histogram has multiple breakdown fields, project all fields except the breakdown field of interest.  
`cat_threshold` |  `int` |  number of categories to keep. In case the underlying cardinality is larger, keep the cat_threshold most frequent categories and create a new category 'other' as the aggregation of the rest of categories. Default is None: keep all categories. Only relevant for string histograms.

#  `` validations Â¶

##  `` DEFAULT_VALIDATIONS `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DEFAULT_VALIDATIONS = [VAL_NEGATIVE_AMOUNT, VAL_ZERO_AMOUNT, VAL_HIGH_AMOUNT, VAL_FUTURE_DATETIME, VAL_PREHISTORIC_DATETIME]
    

##  `` VAL_FUTURE_DATETIME `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    VAL_FUTURE_DATETIME = Validation(desc='datetime in future', semantic=TIMESTAMP, sql_builder=lambda c: count(when(c > current_timestamp(), lit(True))))
    

##  `` VAL_HIGH_AMOUNT `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    VAL_HIGH_AMOUNT = Validation(desc='amount > 1e8', semantic=AMOUNT, sql_builder=lambda c: count(when(isNotNull() & ~isnan(c) & (c > 100000000.0), lit(True))))
    

##  `` VAL_NEGATIVE_AMOUNT `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    VAL_NEGATIVE_AMOUNT = Validation(desc='amount < 0', semantic=AMOUNT, sql_builder=lambda c: count(when(isNotNull() & (c < 0), lit(True))))
    

##  `` VAL_PREHISTORIC_DATETIME `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    VAL_PREHISTORIC_DATETIME = Validation(desc='ancient transaction (<1950)', semantic=TIMESTAMP, sql_builder=lambda c: count(when(isNotNull() & (c < lit(datetime(1950, 1, 1))), lit(True))))
    

##  `` VAL_ZERO_AMOUNT `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    VAL_ZERO_AMOUNT = Validation(desc='amount == 0', semantic=AMOUNT, sql_builder=lambda c: count(when(isNotNull() & (c == 0), lit(True))))

##  `` Validation Â¶
    
    
    Validation(desc: str, semantic: Tag, sql_builder: Callable[[Column], Column], warning_fn: Callable[[int], bool] = lambda x: x > 0)
    

Bases: `object`

Class that represents a descriptive validation to be computed. These validations will be computed in a map and reduce approach.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `desc` Â¶

###  `sql_builder` Â¶

|  `Callable[[Column], Column]` |  Factory that receives a Spark `Column` and returns the Spark SQL validation expression |  _required_  
  
###  `warning_fn` Â¶

|  `Callable[[int], bool]` |  the function to be used to asses when a validation should be alerted. |  `lambda x: x > 0`

#  `` fair_gbm Â¶

##  `` FairGBMConfig Â¶
    
    
    FairGBMConfig(constraint_group_column: str = '', constraint_type: Optional[str] = 'FPR', global_constraint_type: Optional[str] = 'FPR,FNR', global_target_fpr: Optional[float] = 0.05, global_target_fnr: Optional[float] = 0.5, objective: Optional[str] = 'constrained_cross_entropy', constraint_fpr_threshold: Optional[float] = 0.0, constraint_fnr_threshold: Optional[float] = 0.0, multiplier_learning_rate: Optional[float] = 1000.0, init_multipliers: Optional[str] = '', constraint_stepwise_proxy: Optional[str] = 'cross_entropy', objective_stepwise_proxy: Optional[str] = '', **kwargs)
    

Bases: `[GenericLightGBMConfig](../generic_light_gbm/#ds_api.models.generic_light_gbm.GenericLightGBMConfig "            GenericLightGBMConfig \(ds_api.models.generic_light_gbm.GenericLightGBMConfig\)")`

The FairGBM (LightGBM with fairness constraints) configuration object used for model training.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `constraint_group_column` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Sensitive group column. Must be categorical. |  `''`  
  
###  `constraint_type` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Constraint type. Either "FPR", "FNR", "FPR,FNR". |  `'FPR'`  
  
###  `global_constraint_type` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Global constraint type. Either "FPR", "FNR", "FPR,FNR". |  `'FPR,FNR'`  
  
###  `global_target_fpr` Â¶

|  `float` |  (Fairness) Global target FPR. |  `0.05`  
  
###  `global_target_fnr` Â¶

|  `float` |  (Fairness) Global target FNR. |  `0.5`  
  
###  `objective` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Objective function. Either "constrained_cross_entropy", "constrained_recall_objective". |  `'constrained_cross_entropy'`  
  
###  `constraint_fpr_threshold` Â¶

|  `float` |  (Fairness) FPR slack for fairness. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `constraint_fnr_threshold` Â¶

|  `float` |  (Fairness) FNR slack for fairness. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `multiplier_learning_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  (Fairness) Multipliers' learning rate. |  `1000.0`  
  
###  `init_multipliers` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Initial multipliers. |  `''`  
  
###  `constraint_stepwise_proxy` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Stepwise proxy for fairness constraints. Either "cross_entropy", "quadratic", "hinge". |  `'cross_entropy'`  
  
###  `objective_stepwise_proxy` Â¶

|  `str` |  (Fairness) Stepwise proxy for global constraints. Either "cross_entropy", "quadratic", "hinge". |  `''`  
  
Other Parameters:

Name | Type | Description  
---|---|---  
`boosting_type` |  `str` |  Boosting type.  
`num_iterations` |  `int` |  Number of booster iterations.  
`learning_rate` |  `float` |  Learning rate.  
`num_leaves` |  `int` |  Maximum tree leaves.  
`lightgbm_seed` |  `int` |  Seed used to generate other seeds.  
`max_depth` |  `int` |  Maximum tree depth.  
`min_data_in_leaf` |  `int` |  Minimum leaf data points.  
`min_sum_hessian_in_leaf` |  `float` |  Minimal sum of hessian per leaf.  
`bagging_fraction` |  `float` |  Bagging sample fraction.  
`bagging_freq` |  `int` |  Bagging frequency.  
`feature_fraction` |  `float` |  Feature fraction by tree.  
`feature_fraction_bynode` |  `float` |  Feature fraction by tree node.  
`lambda_l1` |  `float` |  L1 regularization.  
`lambda_l2` |  `float` |  L2 regularization.  
`min_gain_to_split` |  `float` |  Minimal gain to split a leaf.  
`drop_rate` |  `float` |  DART drop rate.  
`max_drop` |  `int` |  DART max drop.  
`skip_drop` |  `float` |  DART skip drop.  
`xgboost_dart_mode` |  `bool` |  DART XGBoost mode.  
`uniform_drop` |  `bool` |  DART uniform drop.  
`top_rate` |  `float` |  GOSS top retain rate.  
`other_rate` |  `float` |  GOSS other retain rate.  
`min_data_per_group` |  `int` |  Minimum data per group.  
`max_cat_threshold` |  `int` |  Maximum categories thresholds.  
`cat_l2` |  `float` |  Categorical L2 regularization.  
`cat_smooth` |  `float` |  Categorical smoothing.

`max_bin` |  `int` |  Max bins.  
`min_data_in_bin` |  `int` |  Minimum bin data.  
`bin_construct_sample_cnt` |  `int` |  Bin construction sample count.  
`enable_bundle` |  `bool` |  Exclusive Feature Bundling (EFB).  
`zero_as_missing` |  `bool` |  Use zeroes as missing.  
`is_unbalance` |  `bool` |  Unbalanced label.  
`undersampling` |  `float` |  The value for undersampling. Only this or oversampling can be used.  
`oversampling` |  `float` |  The value for oversampling. Only this or undersampling can be used.  
`seed` |  `int` |  The seed value.  
`ignored_fields` |  `set of str` |  Set of names corresponding to the fields to be ignored when training a model.  
`schema` |  `[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI.  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_config = FairGBMConfig(
    ...     constraint_group_column= "age",
    ...     bagging_fraction=0.9,
    ...     undersampling=0.85
    ...)
    >>> model_config.num_leaves # gets default value of num_leaves
    >>> model_config.num_leaves = 10 # sets num_leaves to 10
    >>> model = model_project.train(
    ...     "model_name",
    ...     datasource,
    ...     "target_field",
    ...     model_config
    ... )
    >>> model.waitUntilModelTrained()
    >>> config_from_model = model.get_config()

    >>> # training an H2O Gradient Boosting Machine

    ...     provider="H2O",

    ...         "max_depth": "5",
    ...         "learn_rate": "1.0",

    ... )

#  `` glm Â¶

##  `` GeneralizedLinearModelConfig Â¶
    
    
    GeneralizedLinearModelConfig(lambda_search: bool = False, standardize: bool = True, max_after_balance_size: float = 5.0, max_hit_ratio_k: int = 0, compute_p_values: bool = False, balance_classes: bool = False, remove_collinear_columns: bool = False, missing_values_handling: str = 'MeanImputation', solver: str = 'AUTO', non_negative: bool = False, intercept: bool = True, link: str = 'family_default', beta_epsilon: float = 0.0001, theta: float = 1e-10, hglm: bool = False, calc_like: bool = False, max_active_predictors: int | None = None, prior: float | None = None, max_iterations: int | None = None, lambda_min_ratio: float | None = None, objective_epsilon: float | None = None, gradient_epsilon: float | None = None, obj_reg: float | None = None, undersampling: float | None = None, oversampling: float | None = None, seed: int | None = None, ignored_fields: set[str] | None = None, schema: [Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None)
    

Bases: `[TrainingConfig](../#ds_api.models.TrainingConfig "            TrainingConfig \(ds_api.models.TrainingConfig\)")`

The H2O Generalized Linear Model configuration object used for model training.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `lambda_search` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Use lambda search starting at lambda max, given lambda is then interpreted as lambda min. |  `False`  
  
###  `standardize` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Standardize numeric columns to have zero mean and unit variance. |  `True`  
  
###  `max_after_balance_size` Â¶

|  `float` |  Maximum relative size of the training data after balancing class counts (can be less than 1.0). Requires balance_classes. |  `5.0`  
  
###  `max_hit_ratio_k` Â¶

|  `int` |  Maximum number (top K) of predictions to use for hit ratio computation (for multi-class only, 0 to disable). |  `0`  
  
###  `compute_p_values` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Request p-values computation, p-values work only with IRLSM solver and no regularization. |  `False`  
  
###  `balance_classes` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Balance training data class counts via over/under-sampling (for imbalanced data). |  `False`  
  
###  `remove_collinear_columns` Â¶

|  `bool` |  In case of linearly dependent columns, remove some of the dependent columns. |  `False`  
  
###  `missing_values_handling` Â¶

|  `str` |  Handling of missing values. Either MeanImputation or Skip. |  `'MeanImputation'`  
  
###  `solver` Â¶

|  `str` |  AUTO will set the solver based on given data and the other parameters. IRLSM is fast on on problems with small number of predictors and for lambda-search with L1 penalty, L_BFGS scales better for datasets with many columns. |  `'AUTO'`  
  
###  `non_negative` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Restrict coefficients (not intercept) to be non-negative. |  `False`  
  
###  `intercept` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Include constant term in the model. |  `True`  
  
###  `link` Â¶

|  `str` |  Link. |  `'family_default'`  
  
###  `beta_epsilon` Â¶

|  `float` |  Converge if beta changes less (using L-infinity norm) than beta esilon, ONLY applies to IRLSM solver. |  `0.0001`  
  
###  `max_active_predictors` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  Maximum number of active predictors during computation. Use as a stopping criterion to prevent expensive model building with many predictors. Default indicates: If the IRLSM solver is used, the value of max_active_predictors is set to 5000 otherwise it is set to 100000000. |  `None`  
  
###  `prior` Â¶

|  `float | None` |  Prior probability for y==1. To be used only for logistic regression if the data has been sampled and the mean of response does not reflect reality. |  `None`  
  
###  `max_iterations` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  Maximum number of iterations. |  `None`  
  
###  `lambda_min_ratio` Â¶

|  `float | None` |  Minimum lambda used in lambda search, specified as a ratio of lambda_max (the smallest lambda that drives all coefficients to zero). Default indicates: if the number of observations is greater than the number of variables, then lambda_min_ratio is set to 0.0001; If the number of observations is less than the number of variables, then lambda_min_ratio is set to 0.01. |  `None`  
  
###  `objective_epsilon` Â¶

|  `float | None` |  Converge if objective value changes less than this. Default indicates: If lambda_search is set to True the value of objective_epsilon is set to .0001. If the lambda_search is set to False and lambda is equal to zero, the value of objective_epsilon is set to .000001, for any other value of lambda the default value of objective_epsilon is set to .0001. |  `None`  
  
###  `gradient_epsilon` Â¶

|  `float | None` |  Converge if objective changes less (using L-infinity norm) than this, ONLY applies to L-BFGS solver. Default indicates: If lambda_search is set to False and lambda is equal to zero, the default value of gradient_epsilon is equal to .000001, otherwise the default value is .0001. If lambda_search is set to True, the conditional values above are 1E-8 and 1E-6 respectively. |  `None`  
  
###  `obj_reg` Â¶

|  `float | None` |  Likelihood divider in objective value computation, default is 1/nobs. |  `None`  
  
###  `theta` Â¶

|  `float` |  Theta. |  `1e-10`  
  
###  `hglm` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If set to true, will return HGLM model. Otherwise, normal GLM model will be returned. |  `False`  
  
###  `calc_like` Â¶

|  `bool` |  If true, will return likelihood function value for HGLM. |  `False`  
  
###  `undersampling` Â¶

|  `float` |  The value for undersampling. Only this or oversampling can be used. |  `None`  
  
###  `oversampling` Â¶

|  `float` |  The value for oversampling. Only this or undersampling can be used. |  `None`  
  
###  `seed` Â¶

|  `int` |  The seed value. |  `None`  
  
###  `ignored_fields` Â¶

|  `set of str` |  Set of names corresponding to the fields to be ignored when training a model. |  `None`  
  
###  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_config = GeneralizedLinearModelConfig()
    >>> model_config.remove_collinear_columns
    >>> model_config.remove_collinear_columns = True # sets it to True
    >>> model = model_project.train(
    ...     "model_name",
    ...     datasource,
    ...     "target_field",
    ...     model_config
    ... )
    >>> model.waitUntilModelTrained()
    >>> config_from_model = model.get_config()

#  `` isolation_forest Â¶  
  
##  `` IsolationForestConfig Â¶
    
    
    IsolationForestConfig(ntrees: int = 50, sample_size: int = 100, min_rows: int = 1, max_depth: int = 8, sample_rate: float = -1.0, mtries: int = -1, nbins: int = 2, seed: int | None = None, ignored_fields: set[str] | None = None, schema: [Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None)
    

Bases: `[TrainingConfig](../#ds_api.models.TrainingConfig "            TrainingConfig \(ds_api.models.TrainingConfig\)")`

The H2O Isolation Forest configuration object used for model training.

This is an unsupervised learning algorithm used for anomaly detection. The target variable will be automatically excluded from features during training.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `ntrees` Â¶

|  `int` |  Number of trees in the forest, defaults to 50. |  `50`  
  
###  `sample_size` Â¶

|  `int` |  Number of samples to use per tree, defaults to 100. |  `100`  
  
###  `min_rows` Â¶

|  `int` |  Minimum number of rows in a leaf, defaults to 1. |  `1`  
  
###  `max_depth` Â¶

|  `int` |  Maximum depth of trees, defaults to 8. |  `8`  
  
###  `sample_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  Row sampling rate. Use -1 for automatic, defaults to -1.0. |  `-1.0`  
  
###  `mtries` Â¶

|  `int` |  Number of features to try per split. Use -1 for automatic, defaults to -1. |  `-1`  
  
###  `nbins` Â¶

|  `int` |  Number of bins for numerical features, defaults to 2. |  `2`  
  
###  `seed` Â¶

|  `int` |  The seed value. |  `None`  
  
###  `ignored_fields` Â¶

|  `set of str` |  Set of names corresponding to the fields to be ignored when training a model. |  `None`  
  
###  `schema` Â¶

|  `[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_config = IsolationForestConfig(
    ...     ntrees=100,
    ...     sample_size=500,
    ...     max_depth=10
    ... )
    >>> model_config.ntrees # gets the ntrees: 100
    >>> model_config.ntrees = 50 # sets the ntrees to 50
    >>> model = model_project.train(
    ...     "model_name",
    ...     datasource,
    ...     "target_field",  # Will be excluded from features automatically
    ...     model_config
    ... )
    >>> model.waitUntilModelTrained()
    >>> config_from_model = model.get_config()

Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_config = LightGBMConfig(

    ...     undersampling=0.85
    ...)
    >>> model_config.num_leaves # gets num_leaves: 31
    >>> model_config.num_leaves = 10 # sets num_leaves to 10
    >>> model = model_project.train(
    ...     "model_name",
    ...     datasource,
    ...     "target_field",
    ...     model_config
    ... )
    >>> model.waitUntilModelTrained()
    >>> config_from_model = model.get_config()

#  `` random_forest Â¶  
  
##  `` RandomForestConfig Â¶
    
    
    RandomForestConfig(num_trees: int = 100, max_depth: int = 20, split_criteria: str = 'One vs others', min_instance_split: int = 2, in_bag: float = 0.65, features_per_split: int = 0, undersampling: float | None = None, oversampling: float | None = None, seed: int | None = None, ignored_fields: set[str] | None = None, schema: [Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None)
    

Bases: `[TrainingConfig](../#ds_api.models.TrainingConfig "            TrainingConfig \(ds_api.models.TrainingConfig\)")`

The Feedzai Random Forest configuration object used for model training.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `num_trees` Â¶

|  `int` |  The number of trees, defaults to 100. |  `100`  
  
###  `max_depth` Â¶

|  `int` |  The depth of each tree, defaults to 20. |  `20`  
  
###  `split_criteria` Â¶

|  `str` |  Split criteria for categorical values, can be "All vs all", or "One vs others". Defaults to "One vs others". |  `'One vs others'`  
  
###  `min_instance_split` Â¶

|  `int` |  The minimum number of split instances, defaults to 2. |  `2`  
  
###  `in_bag` Â¶

|  `float` |  The ratio used for sampling data into the training, defaults to 0.65. |  `0.65`  
  
###  `features_per_split` Â¶

|  `int` |  The number of features per split, defaults to 0 which represents sqrt(N). |  `0`  
  
###  `undersampling` Â¶

|  `float` |  The value for undersampling. Only this or oversampling can be used. |  `None`  
  
###  `oversampling` Â¶

|  `int` |  The seed value. |  `None`  
  
###  `ignored_fields` Â¶

|  `set of str` |  Set of names corresponding to the fields to be ignored when training a model. |  `None`
