|  `[Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)")` |  Pass a schema to enable conversion between descs and internal names. By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> model_config = RandomForestConfig(
    ...     num_trees=200,
    ...     in_bag=0.5,
    ...     undersampling=0.98
    ... )
    >>> model_config.num_trees # gets the num_trees: 200
    >>> model_config.num_trees = 100 # sets the num_trees to 100
    >>> model = model_project.train(
    ...     "model_name",
    ...     datasource,
    ...     "target_field",
    ...     model_config
    ... )
    >>> model.waitUntilModelTrained()
    >>> config_from_model = model.get_config()

    XGBoostConfig(reg_alpha: float = 0.0, max_bins: int = 256, dmatrix_type: str = 'auto', skip_drop: float = 0.0, min_child_weight: float = 1.0, tree_method: str = 'auto', booster: str = 'gbtree', col_sample_rate: float = 1.0, max_delta_step: float = 0.0, max_leaves: int = 0, reg_lambda: float = 1.0, quiet_mode: bool = True, backend: str = 'auto', nthread: int | None = None, min_data_in_leaf: float = 0.0, rate_drop: float = 0.0, min_rows: float = 1.0, col_sample_rate_per_tree: float = 1.0, ntrees: int = 50, gpu_id: int = 0, learn_rate: float = 0.3, colsample_bytree: float = 1.0, min_split_improvement: float = 0.0, sample_rate: float = 1.0, eta: float = 0.3, sub_sample: float = 1.0, max_abs_leafnode_pred: float = 0.0, gamma: float = 0.0, max_depth: int = 6, colsample_bylevel: float = 1.0, score_tree_interval: int = 0, normalize_type: str = 'tree', min_sum_hessian_in_leaf: float = 100.0, grow_policy: str = 'depthwise', one_drop: bool = False, sample_type: str = 'uniform', build_tree_one_node: bool = False, undersampling: float | None = None, oversampling: float | None = None, seed: int | None = None, ignored_fields: set[str] | None = None, schema: [Schema](../../schema/#ds_api.schema.Schema "            Schema \(ds_api.schema.Schema\)") | None = None)
    

Bases: `[TrainingConfig](../#ds_api.models.TrainingConfig "            TrainingConfig \(ds_api.models.TrainingConfig\)")`

The H2O XGBoost configuration object used for model training.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `reg_alpha` Â¶

|  `float` |  L1 regularization. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `max_bins` Â¶

|  `int` |  For tree_method=hist only: maximum number of bins. |  `256`  
  
###  `dmatrix_type` Â¶

|  `str` |  Type of DMatrix. For sparse, NAs and 0 are treated equally. |  `'auto'`  
  
###  `skip_drop` Â¶

|  `float` |  For booster=dart only: skip_drop (0..1). |  `0.0`  
  
###  `min_child_weight` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as min_rows) Fewest allowed (weighted) observations in a leaf. |  `1.0`  
  
###  `tree_method` Â¶

|  `str` |  Tree method. |  `'auto'`  
  
###  `booster` Â¶

|  `str` |  Booster type. |  `'gbtree'`  
  
###  `col_sample_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as colsample_bylevel) Column sample rate (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `max_delta_step` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as max_abs_leafnode_pred) Maximum absolute value of a leaf node prediction. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `max_leaves` Â¶

|  `int` |  For tree_method=hist only: maximum number of leaves. |  `0`  
  
###  `reg_lambda` Â¶

|  `float` |  L2 regularization. |  `1.0`  
  
###  `quiet_mode` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Enable quiet mode. |  `True`  
  
###  `backend` Â¶

|  `str` |  Backend. By default (auto), a GPU is used if available.. |  `'auto'`  
  
###  `nthread` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  Number of parallel threads that can be used to run XGBoost. Cannot exceed H2O cluster limits (-nthreads parameter). Defaults to maximum available. |  `None`  
  
###  `min_data_in_leaf` Â¶

|  `float` |  For tree_method=hist only: the minimum data in a leaf to keep splitting. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `rate_drop` Â¶

|  `float` |  For booster=dart only: rate_drop (0..1). |  `0.0`  
  
###  `min_rows` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as min_child_weight) Fewest allowed (weighted) observations in a leaf. |  `1.0`  
  
###  `col_sample_rate_per_tree` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as colsample_bytree) Column sample rate per tree (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `ntrees` Â¶

|  `int` |  (same as n_estimators) Number of trees. |  `50`  
  
###  `gpu_id` Â¶

|  `int` |  Which GPU to use. |  `0`  
  
###  `learn_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as eta) Learning rate (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `0.3`  
  
###  `colsample_bytree` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as col_sample_rate_per_tree) Column sample rate per tree (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `min_split_improvement` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as gamma) Minimum relative improvement in squared error reduction for a split to happen. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `sample_rate` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as subsample) Row sample rate per tree (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `eta` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as learn_rate) Learning rate (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `0.3`  
  
###  `sub_sample` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as sample_rate) Row sample rate per tree (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `max_abs_leafnode_pred` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as max_delta_step) Maximum absolute value of a leaf node prediction. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `gamma` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as min_split_improvement) Minimum relative improvement in squared error reduction for a split to happen. |  `0.0`  
  
###  `max_depth` Â¶

|  `int` |  Maximum tree depth. |  `6`  
  
###  `colsample_bylevel` Â¶

|  `float` |  (same as col_sample_rate) Column sample rate (from 0.0 to 1.0). |  `1.0`  
  
###  `score_tree_interval` Â¶

|  `int` |  Score the model after every so many trees. Disabled if set to 0. |  `0`  
  
###  `normalize_type` Â¶

|  `str` |  For booster=dart only: normalize_type. |  `'tree'`  
  
###  `min_sum_hessian_in_leaf` Â¶

|  `float` |  For tree_method=hist only: the minimum sum of hessian in a leaf to keep splitting. |  `100.0`  
  
###  `grow_policy` Â¶

|  `str` |  Grow policy - depthwise is standard GBM, lossguide is LightGBM. |  `'depthwise'`  
  
###  `one_drop` Â¶

###  `sample_type` Â¶

###  `build_tree_one_node` Â¶

###  `undersampling` Â¶

|  `float` |  The value for undersampling. Only this or oversampling can be used. |  `None`  
  
###  `oversampling` Â¶

###  `seed` Â¶

###  `ignored_fields` Â¶

By not passing a schema, ds-api will assume field names are references to internal Pulse names, not the ones in the UI. |  `None`

#  `` diff Â¶

Module to enable Plans diff creation.

##  `` DIFF_NAME_PLACEHOLDER `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DIFF_NAME_PLACEHOLDER = '{plan_a}_diff_{plan_b}_{datetime:%Y%m%d%H%M%S}'
    

##  `` LOGGER `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    LOGGER = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` PlanDiff Â¶
    
    
    PlanDiff(plan_a: [Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)"), plan_b: [Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)"))
    

Encapsulates the logic to find the difference between two plans.

Note

The generated diff plan allows the difference between two plans to be executed. This means it not only has the diff but also all the relevant ancestors required to make the plan valid, as well as any downstream features that would be impacted by upstream changes.

Let's say feature X changed. We need to include:

  * Feature X's ancestors to allow the computation of X.
  * Feature X's descendants because they need to be updated.
  * Feature X's descendants ancestors to make the plan valid.



Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `plan_a` Â¶

|  `[Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)")` |  Base plan for the difference. |  _required_  
  
###  `plan_b` Â¶

|  `[Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)")` |  Secondary/"new" plan. |  _required_  
  
###  `` features_to_keep `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    features_to_keep: [FeatureContainer](../features/feature_container/#ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer "            FeatureContainer \(ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer\)") = _diff()
    

###  `` new_feature_dependencies `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    new_feature_dependencies: [FeatureContainer](../features/feature_container/#ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer "            FeatureContainer \(ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer\)")
    

###  `` new_features `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    new_features: [FeatureContainer](../features/feature_container/#ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer "            FeatureContainer \(ds_api.plans.features.feature_container.FeatureContainer\)")
    

###  `` to_pulse Â¶
    
    
    to_pulse(name: Optional[str] = None) -> [Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)")
    

Create the diff plan and persist it in Pulse.

If no name is given, it will create a plan named after the two plans used to create the diff: `{plan_a}_diff_{plan_b}_<timestamp>`.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the diff plan. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`[Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)")` |  The diff plan.  
  
##  `` validate_plan_compatibility Â¶
    
    
    validate_plan_compatibility(a: [Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)"), b: [Plan](../../plan/#ds_api.plan.Plan "            Plan \(ds_api.plan.Plan\)"))

  2. Same timestamp field.

Type | Description

    Channel(name: str, schema_model: [JavaWrapper](../../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.JavaWrapper\)"), timestamp_field: str, id: str | None = None)
    

Immutable representation of a Channel configuration for Omnichannel Plans.

Note that this can be instantiated with a `SchemaModel` directly or with a `ds_api.schema.Schema`.

Examples:
    
    
    >>> from ds_api.plans.omnichannel import Channel
    >>> app = ...
    >>> schema = app.pullSchemaByDesc("Cards Schema")
    >>> channel = Channel("cards", schema, "event_occurred_at")

#  `` base_sampler Â¶

If you want to implement a specific sampling algorithm, you should implement the abstract class :class:`BaseSampler`.

##  `` LOGGER `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    LOGGER = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` MARK_FIELD `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MARK_FIELD = 'fdz_sample_ismarked'
    

##  `` MS_PER_DAY `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MS_PER_DAY = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000
    

##  `` S `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    S = TypeVar('S')
    

##  `` T `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    T = TypeVar('T')
    

##  `` TargetType `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    TargetType = bool | int | str
    

##  `` BaseSampler Â¶
    
    
    BaseSampler(seed: int | None = None)
    

Bases: `ABC`

Base class for sampling implementations.

All subclasses must implement the public :meth:`sample` method.

Any additional configurations that your custom sampler needs, should be passed during instantiation.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `seed` Â¶

|  `int` |  Seed for Spark's random number generator. |  `None`  
  
Examples:
    
    
    >>> class RandomSampler(BaseSampler):
    ...     '''Custom random sampler.'''
    ...     def __init__(self, fraction: float, seed: Optional[int] = None):
    ...         self._fraction = fraction
    ...         super().__init__(seed=seed)
    ...     def sample(self, df: DataFrame) -> DataFrame:
    ...         return df.sample(fraction=self._fraction)
    >>> # Initialize the custom sampler
    >>> random_sampler_1pc = RandomSampler(0.01)
    >>> # Run it against some data
    >>> df = ...
    >>> random_sampler_1pc.sample(df)
    

###  `` TIMESTAMP_MS_FIELD `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    TIMESTAMP_MS_FIELD = 'fdz_timestamp_ms'
    

###  `` seed `property` Â¶
    
    
    seed: int
    

###  `` sample `abstractmethod` Â¶
    
    
    sample(df: _TDataFrame) -> _TDataFrame
    

Sample the input data.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `df` Â¶

|  `_TDataFrame` |  Input data source. |  _required_  
  
##  `` full_recovery Â¶
    
    
    full_recovery(marked_df: _TDataFrame, entity_field: str, recover_column_name: str, mark_field: str = MARK_FIELD)
    

Performs full entity recovery over a sampled DataFrame.

That is, it recovers **all** original instances that belong to any of the sampled entities.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `marked_df` Â¶

|  `_TDataFrame` |  Original pyspark DataFrame. |  _required_  
  
###  `entity_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the entity field. |  _required_  
  
###  `recover_column_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the column marking instances to be retrieved. |  _required_  
  
###  `mark_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field marking sampled instances. |  `MARK_FIELD`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  A pyspark DataFrame containing all relevant instances.  
  
##  `` generate_seed Â¶
    
    
    generate_seed() -> int
    

Generate a random seed based on the timestamp in milliseconds.

##  `` optimal_recovery Â¶
    
    
    optimal_recovery(marked_df: _TDataFrame, entity_field: str, timestamp_field: str, recovery_days: int, recover_column_name: str, mark_field: str = MARK_FIELD) -> _TDataFrame
    

Performs optimal entity recovery over a sampled DataFrame.

That is, for a given sampled instance, it recovers the instances belonging to the same entity and falling in a relevant time period. The relevant period of time is a window before the sampled instance, defined by the recovery_days parameter.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `marked_df` Â¶

|  `_TDataFrame` |  Original pyspark DataFrame. |  _required_  
  
###  `entity_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the entity field. |  _required_  
  
###  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of timestamp field. |  _required_  
  
###  `recovery_days` Â¶

|  `int` |  Size of the recovery period. Typically this value should correspond to the maximum window size of your plan's metrics operator. |  _required_  
  
###  `recover_column_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the column marking instances to be retrieved. |  _required_  
  
###  `mark_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field marking sampled instances. |  `MARK_FIELD`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  A pyspark DataFrame containing all relevant instances from the original.  
  
##  `` recover_entities Â¶
    
    
    recover_entities(marked_df: _TDataFrame, entity_fields: dict[str, int | None], mark_field: str = MARK_FIELD, is_optimal_recovery: bool = True, timestamp_field: str | None = None) -> _TDataFrame
    

Performs entity recovery over a sampled DataFrame.

That is, it recovers the relevant original instances that belong to any of the sampled entities.

Parameters:

---|---|---|---

|  `_TDataFrame` |  Original pyspark DataFrame. It should have a column marking which events were sampled. |  _required_

###  `mark_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field marking sampled instances. |  `MARK_FIELD`  
  
###  `is_optimal_recovery` Â¶

###  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  Name of the timestamp field. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---

#  `` random_undersampler Â¶  
  
Implementation of the history-preserving random undersampling strategy.

##  `` LOGGER `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    LOGGER = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` RandomUndersampler Â¶
    
    
    RandomUndersampler(fraction: float | None = None, n: int | None = None, n_positives: int | None = None, target_field: str | None = None, target_value: [TargetType](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.TargetType "            TargetType
    
    
      
          module-attribute
       \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.TargetType\)") | set[[TargetType](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.TargetType "            TargetType
    
    
      
          module-attribute
       \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.TargetType\)")] | None = None, seed: int | None = None)
    

Bases: `[BaseSampler](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.BaseSampler "            BaseSampler \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.BaseSampler\)")`

History-preserving random undersampling strategy.

The random undersampling is performed according to one (and only one) of the following options to be specified: `fraction`, `n`, `n_positives`.

Note

It is not guaranteed to provide _exactly_ the specified fraction/balance/n.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `fraction` Â¶

|  `float` |  Specifies the fraction of instances to be sampled relative to the whole size of the input data. |  `None`  
  
###  `n` Â¶

|  `int` |  Specifies the number of instances to sample. |  `None`  
  
###  `n_positives` Â¶

|  `int` |  Specifies the desired number of instances for the positive class that the sampled data should contain, maintaining the original balance (random sample). If this option is chosen, the parameters `target_field` and `target_value` must also be given. |  `None`  
  
###  `target_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the target field. This parameter must be specified together with `target_value` if the option `n_positives` was used, otherwise it will be ignored. |  `None`  
  
###  `target_value` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of class associated with the positive class in `target_field`. It can be a set of classes to represent that all those classes should be kept complete without sampling. This parameter must be specified together with `target_field` if the option `n_positives` was used, otherwise it will be ignored. |  `None`  
  
###  `seed` Â¶

|  `int` |  Random seed for Spark's random number generator. |  `None`  
See Also

pyspark.sql.functions.rand

###  `` TIMESTAMP_MS_FIELD `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    TIMESTAMP_MS_FIELD = 'fdz_timestamp_ms'
    

###  `` seed `property` Â¶
    
    
    seed: int
    

###  `` sample Â¶
    
    
    sample(df: _TDataFrame, entity_field: str | None = None, filter_expr: Column | None = None, repartition: bool = False, mark_field: str = [MARK_FIELD](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.MARK_FIELD "            MARK_FIELD
    
    
      
          module-attribute
       \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.MARK_FIELD\)"), optimal_recovery: bool = True, exclude_kickoff_window: bool = True, timestamp_field: str | None = None, recovery_days: int | None = None, entity_fields: list[str] | None = None) -> _TDataFrame
    

Undersample the input data via a history-preserving strategy.

When specifying a filter it may happen that after the filter you get much less data than originally. In this case, your final sample might result in less rows than you expected.

For example, imagine you have 10M rows in your raw data, and you want to work with just 1M. In this case, you would instantiate your sampler with `fraction=0.1`. However, if only 1% of your rows are part of your filter (`filter_expr`) then your sample will only have those rows. In this case the final size will be 1% of the original, instead of 10%.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
####  `df` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  Input data. |  _required_  
  
####  `entity_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field for which recovery should occur. After random sampling is done, all instances belonging to the sampled entities will be recovered so historical features can be properly computed. Can ony be provided if 'entity_fields' is None and vice-versa. |  `None`  
  
####  `filter_expr` Â¶

|  `Column` |  PySpark expression to filter events from the random sampling election. A typical example is a filter for scorable events. |  `None`  
  
####  `repartition` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Indicate if the output DataFrame is to be repartitioned to match the number of instances per partition of the original dataset. Default is False, meaning default partitioning. |  `False`  
  
####  `mark_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field that will be created to preserve the sampling history. Events that were randomly sampled will have a value 'True' for this field. Only applicable if `entity_field` is given. Default is "fdz_sample_ismarked". |  `[MARK_FIELD](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.MARK_FIELD "            MARK_FIELD


  
      module-attribute
   \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.MARK_FIELD\)")`  
  
####  `optimal_recovery` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Indicates whether optimal recovery should be performed. If set to False, full recovery will be used instead. Only applicable if `entity_field` is given. Default value is 'True'. If 'True', 'timestamp_field' and 'recovery_days' are mandatory. |  `True`  
  
####  `exclude_kickoff_window` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Indicates whether first time window should be excluded from marking. Default value is 'True'. If 'True', 'timestamp_field' and 'recovery_days' are mandatory. This is typically useful because profile metrics are not correctly computed in this window. |  `True`  
  
####  `timestamp_field` Â¶

|  `str | None` |  Name of the field that will be used to as timestamp. Required if optimal_recovery is True or exclude_kickoff_window is True. |  `None`  
  
####  `recovery_days` Â¶

|  `int | None` |  Size in days of the window considered. Typically, this value should correspond to the maximum window size of your plan's metrics operator. Applicable if optimal_recovery is True or exclude_kickoff_window is True. In the case that recovery day values are provided through 'entity_fields', this argument is ignored. |  `None`  
  
####  `entity_fields` Â¶

|  `list` |  List of field names for which recovery should occur. Alternatively, can be a list of tuples (field, recovery_days) if different windows are to be applied to each of the fields. After random sampling is done, all instances belonging to the sampled entities will be recovered so historical features can be properly computed. Can ony be provided if 'entity_field' is None and vice versa. In the case that a list of tuples is provided, 'recovery_days' is ignored. If 'exclude_kickoff_window' is 'True' then the corresponding window will be the maximum value of the recovery_days provided in the 'entity_fields' tuples. |  `None`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`DataFrame` |  Sampled data.  
  
Examples:

This examples demonstrates the behaviour of the option exclude_kickoff_window.
    
    
    >>> first_timestamp = df.agg(F.min(timestamp_field)).first()[0]
    >>> datetime.fromtimestamp(first_timestamp/1000)
    2018-12-20 09:27:53
    >>> window_size = 7 #days
    >>> df_sampled = sampler.sample(
    ...     df,
    ...     mark_field='ismarked',
    ...     exclude_kickoff_window = True,
    ...     timestamp_field='timestamp',
    ...     recovery_days=window_size,
    ... )
    >>> first_marked_timestamp = (
    ...     df.filter(col(is_marked)).
    ...     agg(F.min(timestamp_field)).first()[0]
    ... )
    >>> datetime.fromtimestamp(first_marked_timestamp/1000)
    2018-12-27 10:15:48
    >>> MS_PER_DAY = 24*60*60*1000
    >>> first_marked_timestamp >= first_timestamp + max_window*MS_PER_DAY
    True

Bases: `[BaseSampler](../base_sampler/#ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.BaseSampler "            BaseSampler \(ds_api.sampling.base_sampler.BaseSampler\)")`

History-preserving stratified random undersampling strategy.

Constructor for StratifiedRandomUndersampler.

The random undersampling is performed according to one (and only one) of the following options to be specified: `fraction`, `balance`, `n`.

Note

It is not guaranteed to provide _exactly_ the specified fraction/balance/n.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `target_field` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the field used for stratification. |  _required_  
  
###  `target_value` Â¶

|  `(bool, int, str or set)` |  Name of class associated with the positive class in `target_field`. This class will be maintained complete. It can be a set of classes to represent that all those classes should be kept complete without sampling. |  _required_  
  
###  `fraction` Â¶

|  `float` |  Specifies the fraction of the negative class to be sampled. All instances from the positive class will be kept. |  `None`  
  
###  `balance` Â¶

|  `float` |  Specifies the desired target balance. E.g. 0.9 means that the positive:negative ratio will be 1:9. All instances from the positive class will be kept, while the negative class will be sampled in order to result in the specified balance ratio. |  `None`  
  
###  `n` Â¶

|  `int` |  Specifies the number of instances to sample from the negative class. All instances from the positive class will be kept. |  `None`  
  
###  `seed` Â¶

See Also

#  `` fit_indexers Â¶  
  
This module implements the API to allow users to fit new indexers by wrapping the needed functionality from the string-indexing library.

##  `` DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES = 10000
    

##  `` DEFAULT_MIN_CATEGORY_SAMPLES `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DEFAULT_MIN_CATEGORY_SAMPLES = 100
    

##  `` logger `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    logger = getLogger(__name__)
    

##  `` bulk_fit Â¶
    
    
    bulk_fit(app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), indexer_name_pattern: str, data: DataFrame, features: List[str], max_categories: int = DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES, min_category_samples: int = DEFAULT_MIN_CATEGORY_SAMPLES) -> List[[PulseIndexer](../pulse_indexers/#ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer "            PulseIndexer
    
    
      
          module-attribute
       \(ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer\)")]
    

Create several PulseIndexers from a given PySpark DataFrame. This is more performant than calling `fit` directly as operations are submitted as parallel Spark jobs. However, make sure there is enough memory in the executors to hold all indexers in memory.

Indexers with no items are not created on purpose as they would have no use. A warning will be issued for each indexer that was empty.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.App\)")` |  Pulse Application where the indexer will be created. |  _required_  
  
###  `indexer_name_pattern` Â¶

|  `str` |  A format pattern to generate the indexer name for each indexed feature. For example: "v18_{feature}_max_cats_ ". The possible pattern placeholders are: \- {feature} : The name of the column being fit \- {max_categories} : The actual `max_categories` parameter value. \- {min_category_samples} : The actual `min_category_samples` parameter value. It will show up in the Pulse Lists' pane with that name and the indexer resource prefix (`ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PULSE_INDEXER_DESC_PREFIX`). |  _required_  
  
###  `data` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  PySpark DataFrame with data to index. Must have the columns listed in `features`. |  _required_  
  
###  `features` Â¶

|  `list of str` |  Names of the columns to index. |  _required_  
  
###  `max_categories` Â¶

|  `(int,)` | 
    
    
             default = ds_api.string_indexing.fit_indexers.DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES
    

Maximum number of categories from the target column to index. Categories with the least number of observations will be filtered out from the indexer mapping until only `max_categories` are left. Any categories left outside this will be left to the "others" bucket. If set to 0 no such clipping is applied. |  `DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES`  
  
###  `min_category_samples` Â¶

|  `(int,)` | 
    
    
                   default = ds_api.string_indexing.fit_indexers.DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES
    

Minimum number of observations that a category must have during fit to be indexed. If set to 0 no such clipping is applied. |  `DEFAULT_MIN_CATEGORY_SAMPLES`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`List of PulseIndexer` |  The created PulseIndexer Lists.  
  
##  `` fit Â¶
    
    
    fit(app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), indexer_name: str, data: DataFrame, feature: str, max_categories: int = DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES, min_category_samples: int = DEFAULT_MIN_CATEGORY_SAMPLES) -> [PulseIndexer](../pulse_indexers/#ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer "            PulseIndexer
    
    
      
          module-attribute
       \(ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer\)")

|  `str` |  Name to give to the generated PulseIndexer. It will show up in the Pulse Lists' pane with that name and the indexer resource prefix (`ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PULSE_INDEXER_DESC_PREFIX`). |  _required_  
  
###  `data` Â¶

Any categories left outside this will be left to the "others" bucket. If set to 0 no such clipping is applied. |  `DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES`  
  
###  `min_category_samples` Â¶

                   default = ds_api.string_indexing.fit_indexers.DEFAULT_MAX_CATEGORIES

Type | Description  
---|---  
`[PulseIndexer](../pulse_indexers/#ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer "            PulseIndexer


  
      module-attribute
   \(ds_api.string_indexing.pulse_indexers.PulseIndexer\)")` |  The created PulseIndexer List.

Title: pulse_indexers - DS API

#  `` pulse_indexers Â¶  
  
Manage Pulse Indexer resources.

##  `` CARDINALITY_TAG_PREFIX `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    CARDINALITY_TAG_PREFIX = 'defaultValue'
    

##  `` DEFAULT_CATEGORY_COLNAME `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    DEFAULT_CATEGORY_COLNAME = 'category'
    

##  `` INDEXER_TAG_DESC `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    INDEXER_TAG_DESC = 'Indexer'
    

##  `` INDEX_COL `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    INDEX_COL = 'index'
    

##  `` MAX_CATEGORIES_TAG_PREFIX `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    MAX_CATEGORIES_TAG_PREFIX = 'max_categories'

##  `` create_from_pandas_indexer_mapping Â¶
    
    
    create_from_pandas_indexer_mapping(app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), indexer_mapping: DataFrame, indexer_name: str, category_colname: str = DEFAULT_CATEGORY_COLNAME, index_colname: str = INDEX_COL, max_categories: int = 0, min_category_samples: int = 0) -> Optional[PulseIndexer]
    

Takes a dataframe with the indexer mapping and creates a PulseIndexer.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.App\)")` |  Pulse Application. |  _required_  
  
###  `indexer_mapping` Â¶

|  `DataFrame` |  Indexer mapping data. Must have a "category" and an "index" column. |  _required_  
  
###  `indexer_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name to give to the generated PulseIndexer. It will show up in the Pulse Lists' pane with that name and the indexer resource prefix (`PULSE_INDEXER_DESC_PREFIX`). |  _required_  
  
###  `category_colname` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the category column name. |  `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_COLNAME`  
  
###  `index_colname` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the index column name. |  `= ``INDEX_COL```  
  
###  `max_categories` Â¶

|  `int` |  Optional Metadata for end-users. Will be encoded in a tag. Might not make sense for libraries that do not support this feature. Maximum number of categories allowed when fitting the original indexer. |  `0`  
  
###  `min_category_samples` Â¶

|  `int` |  Optional Metadata for end-users. Will be encoded in a tag. Might not make sense for libraries that do not support this feature. Minimum required number of samples allowed to form a category when fitting the indexer. |  `0`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Optional[PulseIndexer]` |  The created PulseIndexer List. If it is empty it is not created and None is returned instead.  
  
##  `` create_from_string_indexer Â¶
    
    
    create_from_string_indexer(app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), indexer: FrequencyStringIndexer, indexer_name: str) -> Optional[PulseIndexer]
    

Turns a FrequencyStringIndexer into a PulseIndexer.

Only FrequencyStringIndexer is supported given the output value produced for unknown/"other" values which matches the number of categories in the indexer. Usually that only makes sense for Frequency Indexers.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)")` |  Pulse Application where the indexer will be created. |  _required_  
  
###  `indexer` Â¶

|  `FrequencyStringIndexer` |  Frequency Indexer. Only FrequencyStringIndexer is supported given the output value for "other" generally doesn't make sense for other indexer types. |  _required_  
  
###  `indexer_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the generated indexer in Pulse. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`Optional[PulseIndexer]` |  Created Pulse Indexer List. If it is empty it is not created and None is returned instead.  
  
##  `` generate_indexer_desc Â¶
    
    
    generate_indexer_desc(indexer_name: str) -> str
    

From an indexer name it computes the desc to use. It adds a prefix signaling this is an indexer. That prefix ensures indexers show up after all other resources with alphanumeric names if resources are sorted according to ASCII encoding.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `indexer_name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the indexer. |  _required_  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`str` |  Indexer user-facing description (desc), with indexer prefix included.  
  
##  `` get Â¶
    
    
    get(app: [App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.app.App\)"), *, name: str = '', desc: str = '', include_items: bool = False) -> Optional[PulseIndexer]
    

Returns the indexer list if it exists in the app.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `app` Â¶

|  `[App](../../app/#ds_api.app.App "            App \(ds_api.App\)")` |  Pulse Application. |  _required_  
  
###  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Pass the indexer name (not the internal name!) (without indexer prefix) Either pass this or `indexer_desc`. |  `''`  
  
###  `desc` Â¶

|  `str` |  Pass the indexer desc (with indexer prefix). Either pass this or `indexer_name`. |  `''`  
  
###  `include_items` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Include the indexer items. |  `False`  
  
Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`PulseIndexer or None` |  Pulse Indexer resource if found, or None otherwise.  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`ValueError` |  Raised if either: \- `name` and `desc` are empty. \- `desc` is invalid (misses the string indexer prefix).

##  `` FeatureContainer Â¶
    
    
    FeatureContainer(features: Iterable[[BaseFeature](../feature/#ds_api.plans.features.feature.BaseFeature "            BaseFeature \(ds_api.plans.features.feature.BaseFeature\)")] | None = None)
    

Holds a collection of features while retaining both their "individual" definition, and their definition within the context of a plan.

This is similar to a dictionary that maps the individual feature definition (ie, without the operator context), with the "full feature" (ie with the operator).

##  `` HashableFeature `dataclass` Â¶
    
    
    HashableFeature(name: str, feature: str)
    

Feature representation without its operator. This is useful to define the 'uniqueness' of a feature. A feature is considered unique based on its contents excluding the operator to which it is attached.

Create a HashableFeature from a BaseFeature (Map, Metric, ...).

For this purpose we only want to keep the feature name ("desc") and its definition without the link to the Operator.

Title: metric_feature - DS API
============================================================

#  `` metric_feature Â¶  
  
This module implements the concrete `MetricFeature` that allows the manipulation of metrics in plans.

From an API perspective there are two properties that even though are persisted at the "metric level", they have to be defined and managed at the operator/plan level":

  1. `filter_incomplete_instances`: When true, the output of the Metrics **operator** will exclude instances whose timestamp is older than the period defined by the start of the dataset and the largest window.
  2. `timestamp_field`: Defined at the **plan** level in deployable plans.



##  `` AGG_DEP_TYPE_NAME `module-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    AGG_DEP_TYPE_NAME = 'aggregation'
    

##  `` DEFAULT_FILTER_INCOMPLETE_INSTANCES `module-attribute` Â¶

##  `` DEFAULT_TIMESTAMP_FIELD `module-attribute` Â¶

##  `` MetricFeature Â¶
    
    
    MetricFeature(name: str, groupby_fields: list[str], aggregation: str, window_size: str, is_real_time: bool = True, *, lookup_fields: list[str] | None = None, scheduled_cron_expression: str | None = None, filter_metric: str | None = None, delay: int | None = None, is_tumbling: bool = False, is_infinite: bool = False, main_channel_contributes: bool = True, secondary_channels: dict[str, MetricSecondaryChannelConfig] | None = None, cascaded_scheduled_metrics: bool = False, coalesce: float | int | str | None = None, description: str | None = None)
    

Bases: `[BaseFeature](../feature/#ds_api.plans.features.feature.BaseFeature "            BaseFeature \(ds_api.plans.features.feature.BaseFeature\)")`

Represents a Metric feature in Pulse.

Parameters:

Name | Type | Description | Default  
---|---|---|---  
  
###  `name` Â¶

|  `str` |  Name of the metric feature. |  _required_  
  
###  `groupby_fields` Â¶

|  `list of str` |  Fields to group by. |  _required_  
  
###  `aggregation` Â¶

|  `str` |  Aggregation expression. Aggregations must be written in PQL: "avg","min","max","count","countDistinct","sum","stddev", "last","first","prev",... Fields must be enclosed with the pattern `${field}` eg. `'avg(${amount})'`, `'countDistinct(${MerchantCity})'`, `'count()'`. **Note that "advanced" aggregations are not compatible with Railgun**. |  _required_  
  
###  `window_size` Â¶

|  `str` |  Window expression. Typically, "1 day", "4 weeks", etc. |  _required_  
  
###  `is_real_time` Â¶

|  `bool` |  True = Real time window, False = Scheduled window. True by default. |  `True`  
  
###  `scheduled_cron_expression` Â¶

|  `str` |  If is_real_time = False, a cron expression must be specified to define the periodicity of the window, by default None e.g. `'0 0 0 * * ?'` for a scheduled window running every day at midnight. |  `None`  
  
###  `filter_metric` Â¶

|  `str` |  Filter expression, by default None. Fields must be enclosed with the pattern `${field}` eg. `"${TransactionAmount} > 100 and ${Fraud} != false"`. |  `None`  
  
###  `delay` Â¶

|  `int` |  Defines delay in milliseconds, must be greater than 1000, by default None. |  `None`  
  
###  `is_tumbling` Â¶

|  `bool` |  Tumbling window, by default False. |  `False`  
  
###  `is_infinite` Â¶

|  `bool` |  When True, this metric will be considered a "non-windowed" metric. _Requires Pulse 25.0.89._ **Experimental Feature.** |  `False`  
  
###  `description` Â¶

|  `str` |  Metric description, by default None. |  `None`  
  
###  `lookup_fields` Â¶

|  `list of str` |  Lookup fields, ie the fields used to query the aggregation. By default these are set to be the same as the `groupby_fields`. When different, it means that one can execute a query like: `group by sender -> lookup by receiver`. _Experimental feature._ |  `None`  
  
###  `main_channel_contributes` Â¶

|  `bool` |  When false, the main channel does not contribute to the aggregation. True by default. _Omnichannel feature_. |  `True`  
  
###  `secondary_channels` Â¶

|  `dict[str, MetricSecondaryChannelConfig]` |  Mapping of channel ID to a `MetricSecondaryChannelConfig`. Empty by default, ie no secondary channels configured. _Omnichannel feature_. |  `None`  
  
###  `cascaded_scheduled_metrics` Â¶

|  `bool` |  When True, it indicates this feature is in a plan with cascading scheduled metrics that depend on each other. False by default. |  `False`  
  
###  `coalesce` Â¶

|  `float or int or str` |  Coalesce value for when the metric aggregation is empty. |  `None`  
  
Raises:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`ValueError` |  If is_real_time = False and scheduled_cron_expression is None. If the timestamp field from the schema is not specified in the Plan. If delay is less than 1000 ms.  
  
###  `` aggregation `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    aggregation
    

###  `` cascaded_scheduled_metrics `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    cascaded_scheduled_metrics: bool
    

###  `` coalesce `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    coalesce: float | int | str | None
    

###  `` delay `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    delay
    

###  `` description `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    description
    

###  `` filter_incomplete_instances `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    filter_incomplete_instances: bool
    

###  `` filter_metric `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    filter_metric
    

###  `` groupby_fields `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    groupby_fields
    

###  `` is_infinite `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    is_infinite: bool
    

###  `` is_real_time `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    is_real_time
    

###  `` is_tumbling `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    is_tumbling: bool
    

###  `` lookup_fields `property` `writable` Â¶
    
    
    lookup_fields

    main_channel_contributes: bool
    

When False, the main channel will not contribute to the metric.

Omnichannel

This property is part of the Omnichannel suite of properties.

###  `` auto_rename Â¶
    
    
    auto_rename()
    

Automatically renames the metric based on its configuration.

Default naming: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_{aggregation}_ {aggregation fields}_{window size}_{is_real_time})` eg. accountid_avg_amount_1h_rt. Advanced aggregations will be named: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_agg_ {window size}_{is_real_time})`.

###  `` get_default_naming Â¶
    
    
    get_default_naming() -> str
    

Returns a default name based on its configuration.

Default naming: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_{aggregation}_ {aggregation fields}_{window size}_{is_real_time})` eg. accountid_avg_amount_1h_rt. Advanced aggregations will be named: `lowercase({groupby_fields}_agg_ {window size}_{is_real_time})`

In the case of "advanced" window expressions, the window expression is omitted from the name.

Railgun compatibility

Note that "advanced" window expressions are not compatible with Railgun. Please use only the appropriate set of window expressions.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`str` |  Default name

##  `` BaseOperator Â¶
    
    
    BaseOperator(pyjnius_java)
    

Bases: `[JavaWrapper](../../../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")`, `ABC`

Abstract class to provide additional Python functionality to all Plan Operators.

Notes

When comparing operators we consider two operators to be the same if they are of the same type and have the same name. This allows a more intuitive comparison between plans where we want to analyze differences of "the same" operator. In such cases we need to first identify the "same" operator between plans so that then we can check how their contents are different.

For reference, `BaseOperator.getName` returns the name of the operator as it appears in the UI.

###  `` subclasses `class-attribute` `instance-attribute` Â¶
    
    
    subclasses: List[[JavaWrapper](../../../java_wrapper/#ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper "            JavaWrapper \(ds_api.java_wrapper.JavaWrapper\)")] = []
    

###  `` get_dependencies `abstractmethod` Â¶
    
    
    get_dependencies() -> Dict[[FeatureUID](../../utils/#ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID "            FeatureUID \(ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID\)"), Set[str]]
    

Gets the features and their direct dependencies within the operator.

Dependencies are not recursive.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict` |  Dictionary of FeatureUID and the name of their direct dependencies.  
  
###  `` get_dependencies_by_type `abstractmethod` Â¶
    
    
    get_dependencies_by_type() -> Dict[[FeatureUID](../../utils/#ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID "            FeatureUID \(ds_api.plans.utils.FeatureUID\)"), Dict[str, List[str]]]
    

Gets the features and their direct dependencies (by type) within the operator.

Dependencies are not recursive.

Returns:

Type | Description  
---|---  
`dict` |  Dictionary of FeatureUID and the dict mapping the types and names of their direct dependencies.
